- **New option: `output.dng.lensCorrectionOpcodes: { distortion?, lateralColor?, vignetting? }`** (with `lens.catalogue`, format "dng", whole frame): the catalogue lens's corrections written into the DNG's OpcodeList3 (tag 51022) as optional DNG 1.3 opcodes (flag bit 0 set): FixVignetteRadial (gain fitted to the #584 profile gain det J/E; near-minimax, at most 0.062 % over the whole catalogue; a fit worse than 0.1 % is refused), then WarpRectilinear (one plane, or red, green, blue for lateral colour; exactly `output.lensCorrections`' mapping with the zoom-to-fill folded into kr0). Each switches separately; all off or omitted writes byte-identical DNGs; the RAW codes are never changed. The assumption `output.dng.lensCorrectionOpcodes` records the opcodes, coefficients, magnification and fit error. `serializeSimulatedCaptureDngFromParts` writes the same bytes from parts.
- **New input: `serializeNativeRawDng({ ..., opcodeList3? })`**: caller OpcodeList3 bytes, framing checked; without it the bytes are unchanged.
- **Limits (documented):** some editors apply DNG opcodes automatically with no control; the guaranteed switch is leaving a correction out in Photivra. Spec: Adobe DNG Specification, "Opcode List Processing" (opcode lists from 1.3.0.0).
- **Tests:** `test/dng-lens-correction-opcodes.test.ts` (independent opcode-list parser and the spec's equations: mapping to 1e-9 px and gain within 0.1 % on PV 16 2.8 and PV 300 5.6; byte identity with none; RAW codes and every parsed tag unchanged; all catalogue lenses fit; parts; refusals).
- **Bundle:** capture-worker shape 352,972 B gzip (+1,197 B: entry +412 B, the lazily loaded catalogue chunk +785 B), over the 352,000 B limit, which is not raised; for the integrator and the owner. Display view delta 45,821 B minified / 18,158 B gzip (budgets 46,500 / 18,550).

### #565 Capture: in-focus diffraction PSF quadrature resolves chart-boundary ties differently from the lattice path

- **Fixed:** `simulateCapture` with `lens.diffraction` in focus and `lens.vignetting`, `lens.distortion`, `lens.projection` or camera rotation (the environment PSF quadrature) formed each tap's source point from its own site, while the request without those options (the object-space convolution), the display view and the out-of-focus capture read the scene on the site lattice. A source point exactly on a chart boundary could therefore resolve to the other material: on a 20 × 14 Siemens star at f/11, an identity vignetting profile (every coefficient 0) changed the site next to the centre from 3,560 to 12,663 e⁻. The quadrature now reads each tap's source at its lattice point's own plane point (#548's lattice formula: the image-space provider with a one-tap kernel), so a tie resolves the same way on every path; the identity profile now gives the same expected electrons (within 1e-9 relative, measured ≤ 1e-15) and the same RAW codes as the request without it. With camera rotation under `motion.shutter: "rolling"` (every row has its own instants, so the lattice is read once per instant) it takes the new flag below; the catalogue lens PSF quadrature is unchanged (#588).
- **Which results change:** in-focus `lens.diffraction` captures with one of those lens options, only where a tap's source point lies exactly on a scene boundary (symmetric charts in symmetric frames; for example a Siemens star with radial distortion, vignetting and the four-spot filter changes RAW codes along its diagonals). Elsewhere a value changes by at most rounding (≤ 1e-15 relative; one multiplication and division by the field factor). Site inputs (`request.siteInput`), `request.diffraction.execution` (`"environment-psf-quadrature"`) and the provider evaluation count are unchanged; the `lens.diffraction` assumption adds that each tap reads the scene at its lattice point. No hash lock or golden image changed.
- **Rolling-shutter rotation: lens.diffraction's fast method** (owner decision 2026-11-09, "build both, default to the faster"; the part's `rollingShutterLatticeTaps` flag was folded into the `fast` scheme at integration): with camera rotation under `motion.shutter: "rolling"`, the exact method reads each tap at its lattice point as above, and the fast method keeps each tap's own point. The format default decides (fast for sRGB samples; exact for DNG, JPEG and PNG); `lens.diffraction: { kind, fast }` or the top-level `fast` set it. The fast method reports `diagnostics.fast.applied["lens.diffraction"]: true` and the assumption `fast.lens.diffraction`. No effect without rolling-shutter rotation and in-focus diffraction. Cost about 1.2× (dead leaves 48 × 32 at f/8: 4.0–4.1 s → 4.8–5.0 s; Siemens star 32 × 24 at f/16: 6.8 s → 8.1–8.2 s; interleaved twice). The two readings differ only at boundary ties; with the default the RAW depends on `output.format`, so region renders joined into one frame should set the flag explicitly (docs/QUICK_START.md, "Optics"). No root export added (a new optional field of `SimulateCaptureLensDiffraction`).
- **Cost:** no slowdown measured (scratch benchmark, main against this change interleaved twice, load average 14–25): dead leaves 40 × 28 with vignetting 2.7–4.2 s → 2.6–2.9 s; a 24 × 16 star with distortion, vignetting and four-spot 17–24 s → 16–19 s; rotation 2.2–2.5 s → 2.1–2.5 s.
- **Bundle:** capture worker +197 B gzip in all (351,775 → 351,972 B; limit 352,000 B, not raised): +113 B for the lattice reading, +84 B for the flag after reusing the existing `queryOffsetsSites` in the object-space provider branches (the same values; it saved about 50 B). Display view's addition +4 B gzip (18,153 → 18,157 B).
- **Tests and docs:** `test/simulate-capture-boundary-ties.test.ts` (new; a 20 × 14 star and an odd-width 21 × 14 star: same RAW and expected electrons with and without the identity profile, fails on 2.19.0; the flag's defaults, a DNG, a JPEG and explicit values on a pitch-rotated odd-width star, whose centre column lies on the vertical spokes, and dead leaves unchanged by the flag); docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md ("Ties on chart boundaries"), docs/QUICK_START.md ("Optics", the `lens.diffraction` row) and the #563 test comment (the generated API reference does not change).

### #567 Lens catalogue: feed each lens's spherical aberration and character into bokeh

- **Changed (shipped images):** `simulateCapture` (and the display view, which reads the same PSF) with `lens.catalogue` and `lens.defocus` out of focus now convolves the lens's own bokeh kernel into its PSF instead of the plain disc of 2.17.0–2.19.0: #553's shaped kernel (`createDefocusBokehSiteKernel`) with the lens's blades, its own resolved W040 as `sphericalAberration`, and, for the fast (f/2 or faster) rectilinear wides and normals (at most 50 mm equivalent), a cat's eye. The defocus is referred to the lens's balanced focus (W020 = −W040), so the kernel is #553's for the signed blur σc − b (b = 8·N·W040/pitch). With a cat's eye each of the PSF's 5 × 5 field nodes gets its own field point's kernel (rectilinear chief-ray tangent, distortion neglected). Kernel rows are flipped to the profile's +Y-up convention. A geometric approximation, labeled as such. Requests without `lens.catalogue` + `lens.defocus`, and catalogue captures in focus or without `lens.defocus`, are byte-identical.
- **New (public field, no new root export):** `resolveGenericLens(...).character.bokeh` (`GenericLensCharacter.bokeh`, a `DefocusBokehOptions`): blades (professional 9 at curvature 0.7, consumer 7 at 0.3, circular at the widest aperture), `sphericalAberration` (the resolved W040 in µm at this f-number) and `catsEye` (one clipping circle of the widest pupil's radius, shifted so that wide open the open pupil at the frame corner is 2^−s_o, s_o the lens's optical vignetting in stops).
- **The cat's eye follows lens.catalogue's `fast` (owner speed rule; the part's `catsEye` flag was folded into the `fast` scheme at integration).** The cat's eye makes the PSF vary over the field, so the fast catalogue method would need a much finer field grid: measured 2.2–3.4× slower on the fast path (for example a whole 750 × 500 frame with *PV 35 1.8* at f/1.8: 0.69 s → 2.33 s), no measurable cost on the exact path. The exact method has it; the fast one leaves it out (assumption `fast.lens.catalogue.catsEye`). The format default decides: on for DNG, JPEG and PNG, off for sRGB samples and the display view. Blades and spherical aberration cost nothing measurable and always apply (fast path 0.31–0.42 s against 2.19.0's 0.35–0.39 s on the same regions).
- **Both catalogue methods:** the bokeh is in the PSF profile both read. The exact method interpolates it per site; the fast method resolves it on its field grid (refined automatically where the cat's eye moves the kernels' centroids) with its usual run-time check against the exact chain. Measured: fast and exact agree within 2 % of the peak on the cat's-eye point.
- **Kept:** `lens.defocus.blades`, `sphericalAberration` and `catsEye` stay refused with `lens.catalogue` (the lens sets all three; the message now says so). The combined 16-site limit is unchanged; #564's run path is not used with a catalogue lens.
- **Assumptions:** `lens.defocus` (catalogue) reports `bokeh: { catsEye, method, sphericalAberrationSites, axisOpenAreaRatio }` and its actual kernel radius; `lens.catalogue.character` reports `bokeh` (no longer under `notRendered`); the catalogue PSF profile's `responseIncludes.pupilClippingShape` is true with a cat's eye. Every catalogue capture's assumptions therefore change (its image, RAW and exposure do not without `lens.defocus`): `test/program-line-lens-range.test.ts` re-pins `catalogue50`'s assumptions hash.
- **Tests:** `test/simulate-capture-lens-catalogue-bokeh.test.ts` (values and cat's-eye rule for all 30 lenses; the flag's defaults and its refusal of a non-boolean; exact against fast; on the point grid the *PV 50 1.8* f/4 heptagon's seven-fold outline in the kernel's orientation, its f/1.8 cat's eye's centroid shift and tilt at the top-left point against #553's kernel, and the *PV 200 5.6* soft-edged behind / bright-rimmed in front). Each check fails for the 2.19.0 disc.
- **Docs:** docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md ("Bokeh (#567)", with each value's reason), docs/QUICK_START.md, docs/USAGE.md, docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md; API reference regenerated.
- **Bundle:** the capture-worker shape +595 B gzip (351,775 → 352,370 B; entry chunk +97 B, catalogue chunk +500 B), over the 352,000 B limit by 370 B on this branch alone; the limit is not raised (`test/capture-bundle.test.ts` fails on this branch until the integrator settles the total). The capture-only entry +95 B; the display view's added size +10 B (18,163 B, budget 18,550 B).

### #555 Adjustable sensor characteristics and #535 noise figure

- **New (#555): sensor characteristics under `simulateCapture({ sensor })`**, each off by default (`SimulateCaptureSensorCharacteristics`): `peakQuantumEfficiency` (green EQE at 555 nm, 0.05–0.9; every channel's curve scaled, shape kept), `fullWell` (`{ electrons, link?: "pixel-area" | "none", referencePixelPitchMicrometers? }`; linked to pixel area by default), `readNoise` (`{ lowGainElectrons?, highGainElectrons?, highGainFromIso? }`, the dual-gain switch ISO), `readoutSeconds` (the rolling-shutter skew), `adcBitDepth` (12, 14 or 16; 16-bit RAW and DNG are new) and `fixedPatternNoise` (the body's synthetic pattern; refused by `simulateDisplayView`). Every value set is listed in the new assumption `sensor.characteristics`, with `chargeScale` and `sensitivityOffsetStops` (how far the output per unit exposure moves from the body's ISO rating) and a note when the full well is unusual for the pitch. A request that sets none is byte-identical to 2.19.0.
- **New (#555, #535): `result.sensor`** (`SimulateCaptureSensorReport`, also on `DisplayViewResult`): the characteristics used, the engineering dynamic range log₂(S_sat / √(σ_read² + σ_q²)) at the capture's ISO and per ISO setting (EMVA 1288 Release 4.0 temporal dark noise, limited by the charge capacity and the ADC range), and (on exact requests, `fast: false`, the exports' default; integration decision) `midGrey`, the expected SNR of an 18 % Lambertian gray patch on the green channel at the capture's exposure, split into photon shot, read, dark-current, quantization and (opt-in) fixed-pattern noise, in electrons, as a ratio and in dB. Model figures, not measurements.
- **Root exports:** 3 new types (`SimulateCaptureSensorCharacteristics`, `SimulateCaptureSensorReport`, `SimulateCaptureNoiseComponents`); no runtime export. The result gains the `sensor` field (additive).
- **Docs and tests:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("Sensor characteristics (#555)"); `test/simulate-capture-sensor-characteristics.test.ts` (QE scales the charge, full-well link and unlink, read noise and switch ISO in dark photon-transfer variance, readout skew, 16-bit DNG round trip, fixed pattern, dynamic range against the formula, mid-grey figure against the measured variance of a uniform patch, its change with exposure and ISO, the display view); API reference regenerated.
- **Bundle:** the capture-worker shape grows by 2,157 B gzip (351,775 → 353,932 B, over the 352,000 B limit), all in the new options' code and the report that `simulateCapture` computes on every capture; see the part report.

## 2.19.0 — published November 9, 2026

A minor release of six issues: exposure time with an ND filter and generic ND presets (#578), the program line within the lens's f-number range (#583), very fast `lens.ideal` ranges (#585), developing a capture rendered in parts (#571), lens corrections in the development (#570) and the sampled-PSF orientation fix (#566). See the [2.19.0 release record](https://github.com/Photivra/photivra/blob/main/docs/RELEASE_2_19_0.md).

- **New:** `calculateFilteredExposureTime` and the generic ND presets `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_PROFILES` / `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_STOPS` (#578); `simulateCaptureFromParts` (#571); `output.lensCorrections` (#570); the progress stages `"diffraction-correction"` and `"sharpening"` (#571).
- **Changed:** program mode limits the generic program line to a lens range that does not hold f/4–f/16 instead of refusing it (#583); a `lens.ideal` range narrower than about f/1 now resolves in every exposure mode (#585).
- **Breaking fix (#566):** the rotationally symmetric `LensSampledPsfProfile` interpolation no longer mirrors asymmetric kernels in y (caller-supplied profiles in that mode only; no built-in lens uses it).
- **Root exports:** 10 new, 1,850 → 1,860 (runtime exports 626 → 631): 8 from #578 (4 runtime), 1 from #571 (runtime), 1 from #570 (type). `GENERIC_LENS_CORRECTION_CHANNEL_WAVELENGTHS_NM` moves module (same value, still a root export).
- **Why minor:** new root exports and opt-in options; requests that 2.18.0 refused now resolve (#583, #585). #566 changes results only for caller-supplied profiles in the rotationally symmetric mode, a breaking fix the owner decided to ship in a minor release (2026-11-07, confirmed 2026-11-09). Nothing is removed, and no default changes.
- **Integration (this release):** `simulateCaptureFromParts` honors `output.lensCorrections` exactly as the whole-frame capture does (byte-identical; a part may keep or drop the option); the lens corrections run between the `diffraction-correction` and `sharpening` progress stages and report no stage of their own. 285 private copies of the same field checks and helpers in the domain parsers (for example `requireRecord` in 44 modules) now share one export each, and 35 identical private helpers found in two modules each are shared too; messages, error classes and results are unchanged.
- **Bundle:** the app's capture-worker shape (every chunk of `NATIVE_RAW_LIMITS`, `prepareDisplayView`, `serializeSimulatedCaptureDngFromParts`, `simulateCapture`, `simulateIdealReference`) is 351,775 B gzip (2.18.0: 351,859 B; limit 352,000 B, not raised). The six parts merged measured 353,811 B; sharing the duplicated helpers saved 2,036 B.

### #566 LensSampledPsfProfile rotationally symmetric mode mirrors asymmetric PSFs in y

- **Breaking fix (owner decision 2026-11-07; confirmed 2026-11-09: a breaking fix, as this entry states):** the `rotationally-symmetric-radial-field-centroid-aligned` interpolation of `LensSampledPsfProfile` rotated each kernel as if its row index ran towards image −Y, while the environment PSF quadrature (`sensorPsfSourcePoint`) applies the row index as image +Y. At field azimuth θ the kernel was therefore turned to −θ: the y mirror of the intended kernel. It now rotates with the quadrature's convention, so an asymmetric kernel renders with the same orientation as a 2-D grid node declared at that field point.
- **Which results change:** only caller-supplied profiles that use this interpolation mode, at field points off the horizontal axis (`fieldYmm ≠ 0`), with a kernel that is not rotationally symmetric (a radial shift such as lateral color, radial elongation, coma, a decentered blur). Their `resolveLensSampledPsf` kernels (and the internal `resolveLensSampledPsfKernel`), and every capture, display view, RAW and convolution kernel built from them (`simulateCapture`, the native environment paths, `calculateSensorPsfIrradianceQuadrature`, `resolveLensPsfConvolutionKernels`), stop being mirrored in y. Kernels on the horizontal axis and rotationally symmetric kernels are unchanged (at θ = 0 and π the old and new rotations agree; a rotationally symmetric kernel is invariant). No built-in lens uses this mode (the generic lens catalogue uses the 2-D grid mode), so every hash lock holds. No API, schema or `src/gpu` change; the model id is unchanged.
- **Tests** (`test/lens-psf-interpolation.test.ts`): an asymmetric kernel (a radially shifted core plus a blob to the counter-clockwise side) resolved in the rotationally symmetric mode and as an exact 2-D grid node, at (±6, ±6) mm in all four quadrants; the light of a point source, placed through `sensorPsfSourcePoint`, has the declared radial (2.25 samples) and across-radius (0.6 samples) moments in both modes, the kernels differ by L1 < 0.25 (bilinear resampling) and the rotated kernel is far from the grid kernel's y mirror (L1 > 1). The test fails on 2.18.0. The test helpers now build kernels with the row index increasing with image +Y; the existing four-orientation rotation test, which used the old convention in its helpers, is unchanged otherwise.
- **Docs:** docs/PSF_FOUNDATION.md states the row convention of the rotation; docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md drops the caveat; API reference regenerated (`LensSampledPsfInterpolation`).

### #578 Exposure time with an ND filter

- **New: `calculateFilteredExposureTime({ shutterSeconds, filter, shutterDialSeconds?, wavelengthNanometers?, wavelengthBasis?, incidentPolarization? })`** (model `filtered-exposure-time` 1.0.0, for the app's Guide page on ND filters and the calculator's ND table): the shutter time that keeps the same exposure at the same f-number and ISO after adding front-of-lens filters, `shutterSeconds / T`, with `T` from the existing filter profiles through `composeFrontOfLensFilterTransmission` (one profile or a stack). Returns `filteredShutterSeconds`, `linearTransmissionFactor`, `attenuationStops`, `exposureFactor`, the full `filterTransmission` and, with a dial list, `dial` (nearest value in stops, shorter on a tie, its exposure error in stops, and `clamped` outside the dial). 1/125 s with a 10-stop filter gives 1024/125 ≈ 8.19 s, 8 s on `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_SHUTTER_SECONDS`; an ND1000 (OD 3.0) gives 8 s. Exact under the linear sensor model (H = E·t, CIPA DC-004-2020 §5.5, EMVA 1288 §2); reciprocity failure is not modelled; nominal filters vary, and the envelope is an approximation whenever a profile is. Filters are resolved at 550 nm by default (neutral filters do not depend on it).
- **New: generic ND presets `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_PROFILES[3 | 6 | 10]` and `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_STOPS`** (owner decision 2026-11-09): frozen, unbranded front-of-lens filter profiles (`generic-nd-3-stop`, `generic-nd-6-stop`, `generic-nd-10-stop`) that transmit exactly 2^-N (1/8, 1/64, 1/1024; "N-stop" means exactly 2^-N), spectrally flat (`neutral-linear-transmission`) over 380–780 nm in air, and marked idealized (`scientificStatus: "approximation"`, uncertainty not quantified, evidence note "Idealized"). 1/125 s with the 10-stop preset gives exactly 8.192 s, 8 s on the third-stop dial.
- **Root exports:** 8 new (4 runtime): `calculateFilteredExposureTime`, `FILTERED_EXPOSURE_DEFAULT_WAVELENGTH_NANOMETERS`, `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_PROFILES`, `GENERIC_ND_FILTER_STOPS` and the types `CalculateFilteredExposureTimeInput`, `FilteredExposureTime`, `FilteredExposureDialSetting`, `GenericNdFilterStops`. Pure function and constant data; no capture, RAW or bundle change.
- **Docs and tests:** docs/PHYSICS_FOUNDATION.md ("Exposure time with an ND filter"), docs/USAGE.md (with a runnable example; later USAGE.md fragment indices in docs/validation/documentation-fragments.json shift by one), docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md, llms.txt, API reference task index; `test/filtered-exposure-time.test.ts` (including exact preset values, flatness, freezing and parser round trip).

### #583 Program mode: the program line follows the lens's f-number range

- **Changed (requests that 2.18.0 refused now resolve):** in `exposure.mode: "program"`, `simulateCapture`, `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` limit the engine's generic program line (f/16 1/2000 s … f/8 1/125 s … f/4 1.024 s) to the lens's f-number range when the range does not hold f/4–f/16, where 2.18.0 refused it ("Program-line aperture lies outside the resolved equipment capability range."). This lifts the 2.18.0 limitation for `lens.ideal` `widestFNumber`/`narrowestFNumber` (#576; deferred to 2.19.0 by the owner on 2026-11-09), and applies equally to the `lens.prime` and `lens.catalogue` ranges that do not hold the line: the 800 and 1200 mm-equivalent primes, *PV 800 5.6 Pro*, *PV 1200 8 Pro*, *PV 200 5.6*, *PV 300 5.6*, *PV 400 5.6*, *PV 500 5.6*, *PV 600 6.3* and *PV 800 9*.
  - **Model (an approximation; a generic line, not a manufacturer's program):** the line's f/4 and f/16 are clamped to the range, giving the same shape: the aperture held at the range's end and the shutter keeping each point's exposure (t/N², camera equation H = 0.65·L·t/N²), still from 1/2000 s to 1.024 s. Where both lines run, the aperture is the old line's clamped in log2 to the range; beyond the line's ends the resolver holds them as before (Auto ISO raises the ISO; a fixed ISO under- or overexposes, reported by `standardExposureDeviationStops`). Example: f/8–f/32 gives f/16 1/2000 s, f/16 1/125 s, f/8 1/125 s, f/8 1.024 s. The `exposure.programLine` assumption records a limited line's profile (id `"facade-generic-program-line-lens-range"`, with its nodes) (`defaultProgramLine` in `src/facade/simulate-capture.ts`).
  - **Unchanged:** requests with no range, and every lens whose range holds f/4–f/16, keep the line, its assumption and their bytes (pinned SHA-256 digests of samples, RAW codes, exposure and assumptions from 3f9d65b for the tier lens, Auto ISO, `lens.ideal` without and with a holding range, `lens.prime: 50` and `pv-50-1.8`). No root export added; no `src/gpu` change. The app's capture-worker shape measured 351,989 B gzip with this part alone (2.18.0: 351,859 B; limit 352,000 B, not raised).
  - **Owner decision (2026-11-09):** keep the line's 1/2000–1.024 s span; the limited line applies to `lens.prime` and `lens.catalogue` ranges as well as `lens.ideal`.
  - **Tests:** `test/program-line-lens-range.test.ts` (byte pins; the limited line equals the old line clamped to the range at the same exposure at 201 points for ten ranges, from 1/2000 s to 1.024 s; program resolves within the range for seven ideal ranges at 5, 2000 and 200,000 lx; the 800 mm prime and *PV 800 9*); `test/ideal-lens.test.ts` updated (the refusal is now an exposure).
  - **Docs:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("Program with a range"), docs/USAGE.md ("The capture facade's program line and the lens's f-number range").

### #585 lens.ideal: a range narrower than about f/1 is refused in every exposure mode

- **Fix (requests that 2.18.0 refused now resolve):** a `lens.ideal` range whose narrowest f-number N0 is below about f/1 (for example f/0.5–f/0.9) was refused in every exposure mode ("referenceExposure.shutterSeconds lies outside the resolved equipment capability range."): #569's exposure anchor at the range's aperture nearest f/8 with the same N²/t needs t = 1/125 s × (N0/8)², shorter than the body's shortest shutter (1/9877 s at f/0.9 against 1/8000 s). The anchor now takes the body's shortest shutter when that t is shorter, a lower N²/t. The anchor is only where the stops are measured from (CIPA DC-004 reference luminance from the camera equation at the anchor, the meter's relative signals and the resolvers all use it), so exposures are the same as with an f/8 anchor: aperture and shutter priority at f/0.7 and 1/4000 s resolve the open ideal lens's settings to 1e-12. The #583 program line's node stops are measured from the anchor. The `exposure.referenceExposure` explanation says "the shortest shutter at f/N0" in that case. Owner decision 2026-11-09: fix in 2.19.0.
  - **Unchanged:** every request that resolved before gives the same bytes (SHA-256 pins of samples, RAW codes, exposure and assumptions for ten requests, including anchors off f/8 at f/9 and f/4 and a fixed f/1 lens). The app's capture-worker shape measures 351,990 B gzip (limit 352,000 B, not raised); the fix's code was paid for by sharing the range clamp between the anchor, the program line and the f-number check, and by a shorter program-line node table.
  - **Tests:** `test/program-line-lens-range.test.ts` (f/0.5–f/0.9 in program with fixed and Auto ISO, aperture priority, shutter priority and manual; the pins).
  - **Docs:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("An optional f-number range"), docs/USAGE.md.

### #571 Develop joined parts: whole-image sharpening and diffraction correction

- **New: `simulateCaptureFromParts(options, parts)`** (#571, for Photivra/apps#253's "Applying … to the whole image" step): develops the whole frame of `options` from region renders of the same request (the `SimulateCaptureDngPart` records `serializeSimulatedCaptureDngFromParts` takes, joined by the same code with the same checks), including `output.diffractionCorrection` and `output.sharpening`, which a region refuses. The joined RAW is the whole render's RAW, so `image.samples`, the JPEG or PNG bytes, `raw` and `plan` equal `simulateCapture(options)` bit for bit. Differences, documented: `diagnostics.providerEvaluationCount` is 0, `timingsMs` are the call's own (`raw` is the join), `diagnostics.fast` and the `raw.*` assumptions stay in the parts' results, and a new `parts` assumption (`{ partCount }`) records the join.
- **Progress:** `SimulateCaptureProgress.stage` gains `"diffraction-correction"` and `"sharpening"`, one event per output row of the whole image (last event `completedTileCount === totalTileCount`), reported by `simulateCapture` too when those options are on; the stages yield to the host about every 8 ms, so `signal` now cancels them. `simulateCaptureFromParts` reports no `raw` stage. Code with an exhaustive `switch` over `stage` sees two new values.
- **Memory:** the detail stages run row by row in place (`correctDiffractionInPlace`: an (R + 1)-row ring; `renderSdrWithUnsharpMaskInPlace`: each row encoded in place by the native SDR kernel, which takes `maximum` 0 for unquantized output, and a (2r + 1)-row ring of the horizontal pass), with the same sums in the same order: one developed image in memory instead of up to six. At 24 MP the whole `simulateCapture` with both stages peaked at 924 MiB (2.18.0: 3,670 MB, resident set sampled every 50 ms). Captures, files, diagnostics and assumptions of every request without `simulateCaptureFromParts` are unchanged (byte comparison of six detail requests against 2.18.0's build: JPEG, 16-bit, thresholded mask, Display P3 PNG, AHD with auto white balance and a push, a 3 × 2 image with σ = 8).
- **Measured at 24 MP** (6000 × 4000 at 6 µm, slanted edge at f/8 with `lens.diffraction`, 8 parts; Apple M5 Pro, Node.js 24.19.0, one thread, shared machine, two runs; `node scripts/benchmark-joined-development.mjs`): join and develop 1.9–2.2 s and 532–551 MiB peak without a detail stage; with sharpening 3.3–3.7 s, with diffraction correction (R = 3) 3.7–3.9 s, with both 5.1–5.4 s (development 1.2 s, correction 2.0–2.1 s, sharpening 1.7–1.8 s), 1,079–1,080 MiB peak each; the whole-frame `simulateCapture` with both took 10.0–10.4 s. The peak includes the parts' RAW and the joined RAW (7 bytes per native site each) and one 64-bit developed image (549 MiB).
- **Root exports:** 1 new (runtime), 1,850 → 1,851: `simulateCaptureFromParts` (new module `src/facade/develop-joined-parts.ts`), named in the `simulate*` family of docs/API_STYLE.md (orchestration returning a `SimulateCaptureResult`), since "develop" is not one of the documented verb families. Messages of the parts join now read "Part k: …" and "The parts …" (were "DNG part k: …" and "The DNG parts …").
- **Decisions (coordinator, 2026-11-09):** the name `simulateCaptureFromParts`; the extra Native performance workflow run that the change to `src/output/native-capture-sdr.ts` triggers is accepted; the two new progress stages count as additive (code with an exhaustive `switch` over `stage` must add them).
- **Bundle (part branch):** the app's capture-worker shape was 351,929 B gzip (2.18.0: 351,852 B; limit 352,000 B, unchanged); with `simulateCaptureFromParts` added to the worker's imports, 351,937 B. The row-by-row stages add about 250 B; reusing the native SDR kernel for the unsharp mask's encoding and sharing the facade's row-progress and wide-gamut encoding code save about 170 B.
- **Tests:** `test/simulate-capture-develop-parts.test.ts` (whole frame against joined parts byte for byte on six requests: JPEG with both stages, 16-bit with a thresholded mask, AHD with auto white balance, a push and the four-spot filter, Display P3 PNG with correction, DNG with both stages, no stage; progress order and rows; refusals and abort; the in-place stages against whole-image passes on odd sizes, R up to 16 on a 12 × 20 image).
- **Docs:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("Developing a capture rendered in parts (#571)", the `onProgress` row, the sharpening section), docs/USAGE.md; API reference regenerated.

### #570 Development option: apply lens corrections to the JPEG

- **New: `output.lensCorrections: { distortion?, lateralColor?, vignetting? }`** (#570, for Photivra/apps#253 story 3.1), with `lens.catalogue`: the lens's generic correction profile (`createGenericLensCorrectionProfile`, #549) applied to the developed linear colour (after demosaic, colour, white balance and `diffractionCorrection`; before the tone curve and `sharpening`), for the JPEG, PNG and `image.samples`. Each correction is off unless `true`; all off (or omitted) develops byte for byte as before. **The RAW and the DNG are never corrected** (tested: identical DNG bytes).
  - **Model** (labelled approximation, the new `output.lensCorrections` assumption): inverse mapping s = q(1 + k1|q|²/R²), q = m·a_c·p, bilinear, one resampling per channel; the image keeps its size and is magnified by 1/m, the smallest that leaves no missing pixel (1 for the barrel wide angles, 1.017 for *PV 300 5.6*). Vignetting is corrected to a flat field with det J/E at the source point, E the lens's relative illumination and 1/det J its distortion's area factor, which the capture applies (flat to 0.03 %; E alone would leave a *PV 16 2.8* corner about 18 % bright). The kernel matches `calculateLensCorrectedCapture` to 1e-12 for distortion and lateral colour.
  - **Regions and parts:** byte-identical to the whole frame (barrel, pincushion with fill, AHD 16-bit PNG). A region develops the frame pixels its corrected pixels read (`developedRect`; `region.nativeRect`, `raw` and `plan` describe that rectangle).
  - **Trade-offs measured through the option** (#552's): distortion correction × 0.706 corner detail (centre × 0.998); vignetting correction raises corner signal × 2.44 and noise × 2.50 (SNR unchanged).
  - **Not offered** by `simulateDisplayView` or `simulateIdealReference` (refused by name); refused without `lens.catalogue`.
  - **Cost:** about 1.3 s per 24 MP frame on one thread (Apple M5 Pro), loaded with the catalogue chunk on demand.
  - **API:** one new root type, `SimulateCaptureLensCorrections`; `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_COST_OPTIONS` gains `"output.lensCorrections"`. `GENERIC_LENS_CORRECTION_CHANNEL_WAVELENGTHS_NM` now lives in `src/output/developed-lens-corrections.ts` (same value, still a root export).
  - **Owner decisions (2026-11-09):** zoom to fill (no missing pixels, no change of output size); the vignetting correction includes the distortion's area factor (the correction profile's own vignetting component, which holds E only, is follow-up #584); the display view refuses `output.lensCorrections` for now; the region rectangles (`developedRect` in the assumption; `region.nativeRect`, `raw` and `plan` for the developed rectangle) are accepted as they are.
  - **Bundle:** on its own the part grew the app's capture-worker shape by 1,735 B gzip (entry +756 B, catalogue chunk +979 B), over the 352,000 B limit; the integration's shared helpers bring the release under it (see the 2.19.0 summary). The limit is not raised.
  - **Docs and tests:** docs/DIGITAL_OPTICS_FOUNDATION.md ("Lens corrections in the development"), docs/QUICK_START.md, docs/USAGE.md; `test/simulate-capture-lens-corrections.test.ts`.

## 2.18.0 — published November 9, 2026

A minor release of four issues: equivalence numbers for teaching (#573), an ideal lens at any physical focal length and f-number (#576), catalogue lenses in the display view (#569) and fast catalogue lens captures (#568). Captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same as 2.17.0 for every request that 2.17.0 renders and that uses none of the new options (every hash lock holds).
- **New:** `calculateSensorEquivalence` and `calculateBlurShareOfFrame` (#573); `lens.ideal: { focalLengthMm, widestFNumber?, narrowestFNumber? }` and `IDEAL_LENS_LIMITS` (#576; the optional f-number range added at the owner's request, 2026-11-09); `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` render `lens.catalogue` (#569); a fast method for `lens.catalogue` behind `fast` or `lens.catalogue: { kind, fast }` (#568).
- **Fixes** (requests that 2.17.0 refused): `subjectDistanceM` may equal twice the focal length, so the Macro's 1:1 close focus renders (#569); a lens whose range excludes f/8 (*PV 800 9*, f/9–f/32) takes the reference exposure at the aperture of its range nearest f/8 with the same N²/t, where every request with it failed (#569).
- **Root exports:** 13 new, 1,837 → 1,850 (runtime exports 623 → 626): 11 from #573 (2 runtime), 1 from #576 (runtime), 1 from #568 (type). #569 adds none.
- **Why minor:** new root exports and new opt-in options; requests that 2.17.0 refused now render (the display view with `lens.catalogue`, a subject at twice the focal length, *PV 800 9*). Nothing is removed or changed incompatibly, and no default changes. No `src/gpu` change.
- **Bundle:** the app's capture-worker shape (every chunk of `NATIVE_RAW_LIMITS`, `prepareDisplayView`, `serializeSimulatedCaptureDngFromParts`, `simulateCapture`, `simulateIdealReference`) is 351,859 B gzip (2.17.0: 347,775 B): #568's lazily loaded fast catalogue code +2,907 B, #569's display-view catalogue PSF +550 B, the entry +397 B (the three parts' option handling), the ideal lens's f-number range +147 B. The integration saves 19 B (one closure for the tabulated paths' tables; unused grid axes removed). `test/capture-bundle.test.ts`'s limit is raised from 348,000 to 352,000 B (owner-approved 2026-11-09 for engine 2.18.0, #568's fast catalogue chunk). The display view adds 45,807 B minified and 18,128 B gzip to a capture bundle (2.17.0: 45,791 B and 18,159 B; budgets 46,500 B and 18,550 B, unchanged).
- **Moved to 2.19.0:** #570, #571 and #566 (the owner's decision of 2026-11-09 to publish these four parts now; #566's breaking orientation fix, decided for 2.18.0 on 2026-11-07, moves with them).

### #573 Equivalence for teaching: equivalent f-number, sensor area ratio and blur as a share of the frame

- **New: `calculateSensorEquivalence({ sensor, focalLengthMm, fNumber, reference? })`** (model `sensor-equivalence` 1.0.0): for a lens on one sensor against 36 × 24 mm (or a `reference`), the crop factor, equivalent focal length, equivalent f-number (k·N, for depth of field, background blur and total light at the same framing and output size), the unchanged exposure f-number, entrance-pupil diameter, sensor area ratio, total-light difference in stops at the same f-number and shutter, and horizontal, vertical and diagonal angles of view. A `basis` record states, for every value, `"exact"` or `"approximation"` and its conditions; the equivalent f-number is a thin-lens approximation, exact only when the focus distance is much greater than the focal length (the exact thin-lens factor k·N·(s − f)/(s − k·f) is stated and tested). 50 mm f/2 on 23.5 × 15.6 mm gives ≈ 76.7 mm and ≈ f/3.07. With the default reference the crop factor and equivalent focal length are bit-identical to `calculateEquivalentFocalLength35Mm`.
- **New: `calculateBlurShareOfFrame({ blurCircleMm, sensor, print? })`** (model `blur-share-of-frame` 1.0.0): a blur-circle diameter as a share of the frame diagonal, width and height, and with a print size, the enlargement (whole frame fitted, long side to long side) and the blur on the print.
- **Root exports:** 11 new (2 runtime): the two functions and the types `CalculateSensorEquivalenceInput`, `SensorEquivalence`, `SensorEquivalenceFieldOfView`, `SensorEquivalenceQuantity`, `EquivalenceExactness`, `EquivalenceValueBasis`, `CalculateBlurShareOfFrameInput`, `BlurShareOfFrame`, `BlurShareOfPrint`. Pure functions; no capture, RAW or bundle change.
- **Docs and tests:** docs/PHYSICS_FOUNDATION.md ("Sensor equivalence: f-number, area and blur share"), docs/USAGE.md, docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md, llms.txt, API reference task index; `test/sensor-equivalence.test.ts` (hand-checked values, the thin-lens check of the approximation, validation).

### #576 Ideal lens at any physical focal length and f-number (capture and display view)

- **New: `lens.ideal: { focalLengthMm }` (#576, for Photivra/apps#246, #250 and #251).** An ideal lens, an aberration-free thin lens named by its physical focal length, on whatever sensor the simulated frame is (no crop-factor scaling, unlike `lens.prime` and `lens.catalogue`), at any `lens.fNumber` from f/0.5 to f/128 and focal lengths of 1–4000 mm. f/0.5 is the physical limit of an aplanatic lens in air (the sine condition, N = 1/(2·sin θ′)); the other bounds are engine bounds. Values beyond them are refused by name (`lens.ideal.focalLengthMm must be 1–4000 mm (IDEAL_LENS_LIMITS).`, `lens.fNumber 0.45 is outside the ideal-50mm range f/0.5–f/128.`).
  - **Model:** it replaces the tier lens's focal length and f-number range, as `lens.prime` does (the tier lens's transmission is kept); its blur is that of `lens.defocus`, `lens.diffraction` and `lens.vignetting`, the same models. No minimum focus distance beyond the thin-lens limits (subject at least twice the focal length away, with #569). Shutter priority and program may choose any aperture in f/0.5–f/128. Recorded in the new `lens.ideal` assumption (`{ lensId: "ideal-50mm", focalLengthMm: 50 }`).
  - **Optional f-number range (owner, 2026-11-09):** `lens.ideal.widestFNumber` and `narrowestFNumber`, within `IDEAL_LENS_LIMITS` and widest ≤ narrowest (each defaults to its bound), narrow the range like a real lens's: `lens.fNumber` must lie in it and shutter priority chooses apertures only in it (1/4000 s, ISO 100 in 2000 lx: wider than f/1.4 over the whole range, f/1.4 with `widestFNumber: 1.4`). Program follows the engine's program line (f/4–f/16) and refuses a range that does not hold it, as for `lens.prime` and `lens.catalogue`. The assumption records the range only when given, so requests without it are unchanged. Refused: `lens.ideal.widestFNumber and narrowestFNumber must be f-numbers in f/0.5–f/128 (IDEAL_LENS_LIMITS), the widest no larger than the narrowest.`
  - **Where:** `simulateCapture`, `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` (it goes through the shared request path). Not combinable with `lens.prime`, `lens.catalogue` or `lens.focalLengthMm`.
  - **Tests** (`test/ideal-lens.test.ts`): 50 mm f/1.8 focused at 2.5 m with an edge at 3 m on full frame (7200 × 4800) and APS-C 23.5 × 15.6 mm (4700 × 3120) at 5 µm: the blur is `calculateDefocusCircle`'s 94.48 µm on both; the display view of the defocused edge equals the view of the disc's closed-form edge-spread function within 2.2e-14, and the capture equals the capture of that closed form byte for byte. Same pixels as the tier lens (`lens.focalLengthMm: 50`, f/4; with defocus and diffraction) and as the 50 mm-equivalent prime on a 36 × 24 mm frame at f/1.8. Bounds, every exposure mode and refusals.
  - **API:** new root export `IDEAL_LENS_LIMITS` (runtime). `SimulateCaptureOptions.lens` gains `ideal`. The f-number range check of `lens.prime` and `lens.catalogue` now shares one code path (same messages).
  - **Compatibility:** requests without `lens.ideal` give the same captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions. No `src/gpu` change.
  - **Docs:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("Ideal lens at any focal length and f-number"), docs/USAGE.md, docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md; API reference regenerated.

### #569 Display view: render catalogue lenses

- **New: the display view renders `lens.catalogue` (#569, for Photivra/apps#251 and #252).** `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` accept the 30 generic catalogue lenses at every f-number of each lens's range and down to its close focus, with `lens.defocus` and `lens.focusScatter`, where 2.17.0 refused `lens.catalogue`. The lens's distortion, vignetting and fisheye projection go through #563's lens signal; its PSF is taken per field zone.
  - **Model (an approximation, the new `view.lensPsf` assumption):** the native raster is cut into square zones of ⌈max(W, H) / 64⌉ sites (94 on 6000 × 4000; one site on frames of at most 64 sites a side), and every site of a zone takes the PSF the capture's own resolver gives at the zone's centre, at each spectral node; every tap reads the scene through the lens with its own field factor, as the capture's environment PSF quadrature does. Catalogue fisheyes see zero radiance beyond 90°, as in the capture. `createDisplayViewLensPsf` in `src/facade/display-view-lens-signal.ts`, loaded on demand; the zones' tables are the expected signal's own (`buildTables`, now exported internally).
  - **Accuracy** (per site, against the capture's own site record and PSF quadrature): ≤ 2e-15 with one-site zones; 2.5e-3 with the box aperture, four-spot filter and defocus (the capture resolves the PSF at each query point); on a whole 6 µm full frame at most 0.66 % per site and 1.2e-4 on average over four hard lenses; all 30 lenses at their widest and narrowest f-number at close focus within 1 %. A slanted edge against the box-downsized capture is within the rectilinear test's 0.6-code RMS and 3-code maximum. Siemens-star boundary ties behave as with `lens.diffraction` (#565).
  - **Cost** on the app's request (24 MP, 2028 × 1352 in 96 × 96 parts, one thread, loaded host): part medians 0.2–0.8 s for most lenses (1.9 s for the Macro at f/22) against 50–80 ms without a lens; 1.9–39× the no-lens view per display pixel (docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "Catalogue lenses"). The CPU renders these requests; `gpu: "auto"` falls back, labelled `unsupported-on-backend`. `scripts/benchmark-display-view.mjs` gains `LENS=catalogue:<id>[:open|:<f-number>]`.
  - **Capture fixes** (only requests that failed before change): `subjectDistanceM` may equal twice the focal length (the Macro's 1:1 close focus was refused); a lens whose range excludes f/8 (*PV 800 9*) takes the reference exposure at the aperture of its range nearest f/8 (its widest for *PV 800 9*) with the same N²/t, where every request with it failed.
  - **Unchanged:** every request without `lens.catalogue` gives the same bytes; no root export is added; no `src/gpu` change. `lensGeometry`'s image-space mapping (#548) now honours `outsideImageCircle: "backward-ray"`, which only catalogue fisheyes set.
  - **Docs and tests:** docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md ("Catalogue lenses"), docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md, docs/QUICK_START.md; `test/display-view-lens-catalogue.test.ts`.

### #568 Fast catalogue lens captures for interactive use

- **New: a fast method for `lens.catalogue` (#568, for Photivra/apps#251).** `fast: true` on a `simulateCapture` request, or the record form `lens: { catalogue: { kind: "<lens id>", fast }, fNumber }` (whose `fast` overrides the top-level flag), renders a catalogue lens from tabulated expected electrons instead of the environment PSF quadrature's per-tap scene queries. It is the first option with a fast method under the #395 convention: `lens.catalogue` joins `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_FAST_OPTIONS`, and without the flag the exact method runs unchanged.
  - **Approximation (labeled):** the catalogue PSF is resolved exactly on a field grid (at least 4 cells between neighboring profile field nodes, more where the node kernels' centroids move by over 0.05 site per cell) and each site takes the bilinear blend of its four grid points' kernels instead of its own; distortion, vignetting and the fisheye projection are the exact method's. Reported in `diagnostics.fast` (`applied: { "lens.catalogue": true }`, `assurance: "approximation-with-a-priori-bound"`), the new `fast.lens.catalogue` assumption and `raw.expectedSignal` (method `tabulated-expected-electrons-catalogue-psf-field-grid`). One site per CFA phase runs the exact chain at run time and must agree within 0.5 %, or the exact method runs.
  - **Accuracy against the exact method:** without the blend the sum equals the exact chain's within 1e-9; with it, site by site within 2.3e-4 of the frame's peak and 2.0e-3 of the site's own value (tested bounds 1e-3 and 5e-3); on the #549 charts the edge rise within 0.010 px, coma within 0.0001 px, lateral color within 0.003 px, distortion and vignetting unchanged (tested bounds 0.02 px and 0.005 stops).
  - **Cost** (Apple M5 Pro, Node 24.19.0, one thread; Siemens star, whole full frame, f/8, PV 50 1.8 against `lens.prime` 50): 768 × 512 renders in 0.209 s against 0.145 s (1.44×; the issue's target was 2×) and 185 s with the exact method; 384 × 256 in 0.084 s (exact 47.5 s). `node scripts/benchmark-catalogue-capture.mjs` reproduces it (docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md, "Fast method").
  - **Where:** a still camera, site-center queries (no anti-aliasing filter or box pixel aperture) and a chart or material scene, in whole frames and regions, with or without `lens.defocus`; elsewhere the exact method runs and `applied` is `false`. The display view renders `lens.catalogue` with #569's per-zone PSF and never takes the fast method (`applied` is `false` there).
  - **API:** one new root type export, `SimulateCaptureLensCatalogueRecord`; `SimulateCaptureOptions.lens.catalogue` also takes the record; `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_FAST_OPTIONS` gains `lens.catalogue`. Internal: `renderTabulatedNativeRaw` takes an optional verification tolerance.
  - **Compatibility:** every request without `fast: true` (or the record's `fast`) renders byte-identically to 2.17.0. A request that already passed `fast: true` with `lens.catalogue` now takes the fast method.

## 2.17.0 — published November 8, 2026

A minor release of four issues: large defocus discs (#564), bokeh character (#553), lens projection, distortion, vignetting and rotation in the display view (#563), and the generic lens catalogue (#549). Captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same as 2.16.0 for every request that 2.16.0 renders and that uses none of the new options (every hash lock holds).
- **New:** `lens.defocus` up to a kernel radius of 512 sites (#564); `lens.defocus.blades`, `sphericalAberration` and `catsEye` and `calculateDefocusBokehKernel` (#553); the display view with `lens.projection`, `lens.distortion`, `lens.vignetting` and global-shutter rotation (#563); `lens.catalogue`, `lens.focusScatter` and the 30-lens `GENERIC_LENS_CATALOGUE`, each lens with a short `summary` (#549).
- **Root exports:** 27 new, 1,810 → 1,837 (runtime exports 614 → 623): 9 from #553 (2 runtime) and 18 from #549 (7 runtime). #564 and #563 add none.
- **Why minor:** new root exports and new opt-in options; requests that 2.16.0 refused (discs above 64 sites, the display view with lens options) now render. Nothing is removed or changed incompatibly, and no default changes.
- **GPU:** #564 changes `src/gpu` (the display view's renderers refuse a disc above 64 sites, so it renders on the CPU, labelled); the hardware gates are in docs/RELEASE_2_17_0.md and docs/WEBGPU_DEVICES.md.
- **Bundle:** the display view adds 45,791 B minified and 18,159 B gzip to a capture bundle (2.16.0: 46,380 B and 18,540 B; budgets 46,500 B and 18,550 B, unchanged).
- **Follow-ups filed:** #565 (in-focus diffraction quadrature and chart-boundary ties), #566 (a PSF mode mirrors asymmetric PSFs; owner decision 2026-11-07: fixed in 2.18.0 as a breaking fix), #567 (catalogue character into bokeh).

- **New: large defocus discs (#564).** `lens.defocus` now renders blur discs up to a kernel radius of 512 sites (1,025 sites across, 6.15 mm at 6 µm) instead of 64, in `simulateCapture` (object-space, image-space and tabulated paths) and in `simulateDisplayView` / `prepareDisplayView`. The owner's case, 50 mm f/1.4 on 6 µm full frame focused at infinity with the subject at 0.83 m (a 2,144 µm blur, 357 sites, radius 179), renders where 2.16.0 refused it.
  - **Method:** above 64 sites each kernel row is split into runs of equal weights, and a run's sum is a difference of two prefix sums of the source row (summed-area tables, Crow 1984; Heckbert 1986). The disc's interior cells share one weight, so it costs about 12·R reads per output instead of (2R + 1)² (2,149 against 101,689 at R = 179). It is the same sum regrouped, not an approximation: within 2.5e-14 of a compensated sum on the Siemens star, slanted edge and dead leaves at R = 65, 179 and 512, where the direct gather was up to 9.9e-12 off. Fixed tiles at absolute positions keep regions, parts and DNG parts bit-identical to the whole frame. The tabulated expected signal applies the disc once per channel to the in-focus signal (defocus commutes with the spectral, offset and diffraction sums); with `lens.diffraction` the providers apply each node's diffraction kernel after the disc.
  - **Cost** (Apple M5 Pro, Node 24.19.0, shared machine): a 1024 × 683 Siemens-star capture at 6 µm took 0.72–0.77 s at R = 65, 1.6–1.7 s at R = 179 and 5.4–5.7 s at R = 512 (peak memory 216–457 MiB), against 5.7–6.8 s at R = 64 (direct). The app's display view (24 MP, 2028 × 1352, Speed) extrapolates to 98 s at R = 179 and 296 s at R = 512 on one thread, against 431 s at R = 64. `node scripts/benchmark-large-defocus.mjs` reproduces these.
  - **Unchanged:** every request with a disc radius of 64 sites or less renders byte-identically to 2.16.0 (the direct convolution, the same assumptions; all hash locks hold).
  - **API:** `DEFOCUS_DISC_KERNEL_MAXIMUM_RADIUS_SITES` is 512 (was 64). `calculateDefocusDiscKernelRadius` (model 1.1.0) reports the new limit and gains `convolution` (`"direct"`, `"runs"`, or null past the limit). The `lens.defocus` and `view.defocus` assumptions gain `convolution: "runs"` above 64 sites only. No root export is added or removed.
  - **Errors:** a blur past the limit fails with a short message, for example `lens.defocus: The blur circle is 7.81 mm (1,303 sites) across; the engine renders at most 1,025 sites (DEFOCUS_DISC_KERNEL_MAXIMUM_RADIUS_SITES).`
  - **GPU (`src/gpu`):** the display view's Speed and Quality renderers refuse a disc above 64 sites (a new `DisplayViewGpuRequest.largeDefocus` and a render-time check), so `gpu: "auto"` renders it on the CPU, labelled `unsupported-on-backend`. In-focus and ≤ 64-site requests reach the GPU as before; no kernel or WGSL changed.
  - **Bokeh (#553):** the run convolution takes the shaped kernel when bokeh options are given (blade polygons share the disc's uniform interior). A kernel needing more than 16,641 reads per site (spherical aberration's ray binning, above 64 sites) is refused with `lens.defocus: this blur needs … reads per site; above a kernel radius of 64 sites the limit is 16,641. Reduce the spherical aberration or the defocus.` A cat's eye above 64 sites is refused (this path takes one kernel per request). See "Integration" below.
  - **Docs:** docs/QUICK_START.md ("Large defocus discs"), docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, docs/USAGE.md, docs/WEBGPU.md; API reference regenerated. Tests: `test/site-kernel-runs.test.ts`, `test/large-defocus.test.ts`, and updated limit tests.
- **New: bokeh character, the defocus kernel shaped by the lens (#553, for #549).** `lens.defocus` takes three optional parts, each off by default: `blades` `{ count, firstBladeEdgeAngleDegrees?, curvature?, circularAtOrBelowFNumber? }` (a regular or rounded-blade polygon, scaled to the f/N circle's area), `sphericalAberration` `{ w040Micrometers, atFNumber }` (primary W040, scaled to the working f-number as (N₀/N)⁴) and `catsEye` `{ apertures: [{ radiusMm, shiftPerTanFieldMm }], fieldZonesAcrossLongSide? }` (mechanical vignetting, `calculateMechanicalVignetting`'s model).
  - **Model** (a geometric approximation, labelled in the `lens.defocus` assumption's new `bokeh` record): the open pupil projected onto the sensor by the ray map (σ·c/2 + b·ρ²)·P, with c the thin-lens blur diameter, σ = +1 behind the focus plane and −1 in front, and b = 8·N·W040/pitch the transverse ray aberration (Wyant & Creath 1992, free). Front and back blurs are the same pupil turned through 180°; with undercorrected spherical aberration the blur behind the focus plane is soft-edged and the one in front bright-rimmed (reversed when overcorrected). Without spherical aberration each weight is the exact polygon area in the site's cell; with it, rays are binned per cell. Bit-identical in every JavaScript engine.
  - **Where:** `simulateCapture` (tabulated, object-space and image-space paths) renders all three; the display view (CPU and GPU) renders `blades` and `sphericalAberration`. Above a kernel radius of 64 sites the shaped kernel takes #564's run path (CPU only); a shaped kernel past 512 sites is refused with #564's short message. The cat's eye is applied per square field zone (a piecewise-constant approximation whose largest step is reported), leaves the tabulated fast path, does not apply the light it removes (declare it with `lens.vignetting`), and is refused with `lens.diffraction` and in the display view.
  - **API:** new root exports `calculateDefocusBokehKernel`, `DEFOCUS_BOKEH_MAXIMUM_BLADES` and the types `CalculateDefocusBokehKernelInput`, `DefocusBokehKernel`, `DefocusBokehOptions`, `DefocusBokehBlades`, `DefocusBokehSphericalAberration`, `DefocusBokehCatsEye`, `DefocusBokehClippingAperture` (9 new, 2 runtime). `SimulateCaptureOptions.lens.defocus` gains the three optional fields.
  - **Compatibility:** requests without the new fields, and in-focus requests with them, give the same captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions (`lens.defocus` keeps the exact disc). No `src/gpu` change.
  - **Tests:** `test/defocus-bokeh-kernel.test.ts` (polygon area and shape against a supersampled closed-form polygon; cat's eye against `calculateMechanicalVignetting`'s exact area at 0–25°; the spherical-aberration encircled energy and rim profile against the analytic ray map; front against back) and `test/simulate-capture-defocus-bokeh.test.ts` (visible on the point grid out of focus; cat's eye by field zone; byte-identity; regions; validation; the GPU display view's float32 emulation). Numbers and cost: docs/QUICK_START.md, "Bokeh character".
- **Display view: lens projection, distortion, vignetting and camera rotation (#563, for Photivra/apps#252; with #548, #549 and #553).** `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` now render `lens.projection`, `lens.distortion`, `lens.vignetting` and camera rotation (`motion.angularVelocityRadPerSec`, global shutter), in focus and with `lens.defocus` out of focus, the combinations `simulateCapture` renders since #548. Until now they refused them in any focus.
  - **Model:** each site's expected electrons are the capture environment chain's sum, μ_c = Σ_o w_o·(1/T)Σ_t Σ_λ A_c(λ) Σ_k K_λ(k)·F(s)·L(D_t(s), λ): the query point's (or, with diffraction or defocus, each lattice source point's) scene ray through the inverse distortion, the projection and the rotation at each temporal midpoint, times its field factor (vignetting × distortion area factor), with the view's own combined kernels and material tables. In focus it is the environment's per-query sum (with `lens.diffraction`, its PSF quadrature); out of focus it is #548's image-space disc, and the view records the capture's `lens.defocus` assumption. The estimators, noise, AHD, development and highlight count are unchanged (`src/facade/display-view-lens-signal.ts`, loaded on demand; docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "Lens projection, distortion, vignetting and rotation").
  - **Accuracy:** every site's expected electrons equal the capture's within 1e-9 relative (the rectilinear view's tolerance) in eight in-focus and two out-of-focus combinations. Exception: with `lens.diffraction` in focus, a lattice source point exactly on a chart boundary (a Siemens star's diagonals through a symmetric frame's centre) is rounded as the lattice rounds it, as the rectilinear view and the out-of-focus capture do; the in-focus capture's PSF quadrature can resolve it to the other material, as it does against its own rectilinear path. Against box-downsized captures (a slanted edge through distortion and vignetting, in and out of focus) within the rectilinear test's 0.6-code RMS and 3-code maximum. On 6000 × 4000 blocks at the frame's corner (4 real pixels per display pixel, a checkerboard, ISO 100), the view's expected value differs from the three-seed capture mean by 0.25–0.29 codes RMS (at most 1.36), no more than the reference's own noise (0.30–0.34), and by at most 0.04 code in each channel's mean, over Speed and Quality with distortion and vignetting, a fisheye, rotation (Speed) and defocus.
  - **Still refused:** rotation with `motion.shutter: "rolling"`, and a mapping or vignetting profile that does not reach the outer site centres plus the kernel radius and the largest query offset (the message names the reach).
  - **GPU:** these requests stay on the CPU reference: `gpu: "auto"` falls back with `unsupported-on-backend` before any device is requested (the limitation names #563), `"require"` fails with it. `src/gpu` is unchanged, so no new hardware evidence is needed.
  - **Cost:** on the app request (2028 × 1352 in 96 × 96 parts, f/8, Apple M5 Pro under a load average of 10–33, so ratios are the measure), µs per display pixel against the rectilinear view: Speed 5.7–6.3 with vignetting (1.2×), 7.2–7.8 with an equisolid fisheye (1.5×), 18.2–19.3 with distortion and vignetting (3.8×, the capture's own inverse-distortion bisection per point), 30.4–32.7 with rotation at eight midpoints (6.5×), against 4.6–5.2; Quality 41.8–44.1 (5.2×), 37.5 (4.5×) and 54.8–56.5 (6.7×) against 8.1–8.4 (every kernel tap carries its own field factor, so the one-material shortcut does not apply). Quality with rotation is refused by the capture's own limit of 100 000 scene queries per site. Prepare times unchanged.
  - **Bundle:** the display view's addition to a capture bundle is 46,341 B minified and 18,527 B gzip, down from 46,380 B and 18,540 B (budget 46,500 B and 18,550 B, unchanged): a simpler Kelvin white-balance branch and one view-region expression in `simulate-display-view.ts` pay for the lens hook. The lens module is a separate chunk (4,380 B, 2,173 B gzip) loaded only for these options; the capture-only entry grows by 642 B (212 B gzip) for the request setup in `runCapture`, and a bundler emits that chunk there too, never loaded.
  - **Internal:** `prepareIlluminationVignettingThroughput` (not exported from the package) evaluates `calculateIlluminationVignetting`'s throughput with the profile validated once; #548's image-space geometry uses it, with the same numbers. `DisplayViewGpuRequest` gains `lens`. `scripts/benchmark-display-view.mjs --app` gains `LENS` variants.
  - **Compatibility:** no new root export; every request that renders today gives the same bytes (hash locks unchanged). Tests: `test/display-view-lens.test.ts`; `test/display-view.test.ts` and `test/simulate-capture-defocus-optics.test.ts` updated for the refusals that are gone.
- **New: the generic lens catalogue, 30 primes with documented optical character (#549, for the apps "Pro and consumer lens sets" epic).** Two sets, Professional (17: *PV 8 3.5 Fisheye Pro* to *PV 1200 8 Pro*) and Consumer (13: *PV 16 2.8* to *PV 800 9*), named `PV <focal length> <maximum aperture>` with ids such as `pv-50-1.2-pro`, `pv-50-1.8`, `pv-100-2.8-macro-pro`. Every lens is Photivra's own generic design, not modelled on any product; every number is a labelled approximation (docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md).
  - **Lenses:** the owner's table of focal lengths and widest apertures; the generic smallest aperture (f/16 to 16 mm, f/22 to 135 mm, f/32 beyond) and close focus (0.15×, 1:1 for the macro, at least 10 cm); equisolid fisheyes (8 mm circular, 15 mm full frame). Focal lengths scale by the crop factor, as `lens.prime`.
  - **Character:** a set baseline (the owner's table) times per-lens adjustments, each with its reason: Seidel spherical (balanced by defocus), coma, astigmatism and field curvature at the corner, scaled with the pupil (a⁴, a³, a², a², a = N0/N) and a close-focus factor; axial colour (defocus) and lateral colour (radial shift) with the normalised Cauchy dispersion; radial distortion; vignetting E = (1 − bρ²)² with optical vignetting fading over two stops; flare, focus breathing and focus scatter as numbers (Hopkins 1950; Wyant & Creath 1992, free).
  - **`simulateCapture` `lens.catalogue`:** renders the lens's PSF (scalar Fraunhofer diffraction of the aberrated pupil by FFT, integrated over site cells, on a 5 × 5 field grid × 5 wavelengths, centroid-aligned interpolation, window keeping all but ≤ 3 % of each node's energy) through the environment PSF quadrature, its distortion as the lens field mapping, its vignetting as field throughput and the fisheyes' projection. Directions at or beyond 90° see no scene, so the circular fisheye's image ends at its 180° circle (opt-in `zeroBeyondHorizon` in the facade's provider, and `EnvironmentLensFieldMapping.outsideImageCircle: "backward-ray"` for points outside the equisolid image circle). `lens.defocus` convolves the disc into the PSF (combined radius up to 16 sites); `lens.focusScatter: { seed, shotIndex? }` applies the seeded autofocus error, off by default. Recorded in the assumptions `lens.catalogue` and `lens.catalogue.character`; `diagnostics.options` gains `lens.catalogue` (`SIMULATE_CAPTURE_COST_OPTIONS` lists it). Not combinable with `prime`, `focalLengthMm`, `projection`, `distortion`, `vignetting`, `psf`, `diffraction` or `pupilSampling`; the display view refuses it. The catalogue and its PSF builder are loaded on demand (`src/facade/generic-lens-capture.ts`).
  - **Measured on the charts** (`test/simulate-capture-lens-catalogue.test.ts`, *PV 24 1.4 Pro* against *PV 24 1.8* at their widest apertures, full frame): slanted-edge 10–90 % rise 0.91 / 1.09 px against 1.01 / 1.44 px (centre / corner, 6 µm); corner coma (point, wide open minus f/5.6, 3 µm) 0.02 px against 0.36 px; corner lateral colour 0.31 px against 0.71 px; grid-line bow 0.00 px (expected 0.31) against 0.60 px (expected 0.92) at 120 µm; corner vignetting 1.15 against 1.64 stops, 0.74 at f/5.6, each within 0.02 stops of the profile with the distortion's area factor; the 8 mm's black surround and the 15 mm's filled frame. The PSF builder matches the engine's diffraction-limited site kernels without aberrations (to 0.004 per cell), samples every lens's wavefront within Nyquist (wide open, minimum focus, corner, 380 nm) and points coma and the blue image outward in every quadrant (`test/generic-lens-catalogue.test.ts`).
  - **Focus consistency:** `simulateGenericLensFocusError` (seeded, reproducible; σ = 0.1 / 0.3 of the depth of focus 2·N·c for the sets' baselines, tested over 4000 shots).
  - **Correction profiles:** `createGenericLensCorrectionProfile` builds each lens's generic correction profile (distortion map, per-channel lateral-colour scale, vignetting gain) for `resolveLensCorrectionPlan`; `simulateCapture` does not apply it.
  - **Cost:** like `lens.distortion`, one scene query per kernel tap through the lens field mapping: a 48 × 48 region of a full frame at 6 µm takes 1.2–1.3 s (pro, 3 × 3 kernel) to 5.9–9.7 s (consumer, 7 × 7 to 9 × 9) at the default spectral step, against 0.01 s with `lens.prime` (Apple M5 Pro, Node.js 24.19.0).
  - **Found, left as is:** the sampled-PSF mode `rotationally-symmetric-radial-field-centroid-aligned` rotates kernels as if rows ran towards −Y, while the environment PSF quadrature (`sensorPsfSourcePoint`) applies rows as +Y, so an asymmetric kernel under that mode renders mirrored in y. The catalogue uses the 2-D grid mode; changing either would change existing captures. Owner decision 2026-11-07: fixed in 2.18.0, as a breaking fix with an orientation test (#566); 2.17.0 leaves it as is.
  - **API:** 18 new root exports (7 runtime): `GENERIC_LENS_CATALOGUE`, `GENERIC_LENS_IDS`, `GENERIC_LENS_SET_BASELINES`, `resolveGenericLens`, `simulateGenericLensFocusError`, `createGenericLensCorrectionProfile`, `GENERIC_LENS_CORRECTION_CHANNEL_WAVELENGTHS_NM` and the types `GenericLensCatalogueEntry`, `GenericLensSet`, `GenericLensKind`, `GenericLensCharacterValues`, `GenericLensAdjustments`, `GenericLensCharacter`, `ResolvedGenericLens`, `ResolveGenericLensInput`, `SimulateGenericLensFocusErrorInput`, `GenericLensFocusError`, `CreateGenericLensCorrectionProfileInput`. Each `GenericLensCatalogueEntry` and `ResolvedGenericLens` has `summary`, a short plain-English description of the lens's character (at most 40 characters, US spelling, written from its adjustments and reason; tested against them). `SimulateCaptureOptions.lens` gains `catalogue` and `focusScatter`; `EnvironmentLensFieldMapping` gains the optional `outsideImageCircle`. Root exports 1,810 → 1,828 (runtime 614 → 621).
  - **Compatibility:** requests without `lens.catalogue` give the same captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions; a lens field mapping without `outsideImageCircle` still refuses points outside the image circle. The display view's added bundle size is 46,366 B minified and 18,498 B gzip (2.16.0: 46,380 B and 18,540 B; budgets unchanged). No `src/gpu` change.
  - **Not in 2.17.0:** a catalogue lens's bokeh character. #553's bokeh options are refused with `lens.catalogue` (the lens folds a plain disc into its PSF), and feeding each lens's spherical aberration and character into the bokeh kernel is a follow-up (#567). The display view still refuses `lens.catalogue`. The phone profile's three fixed lenses are #554. Docs: docs/LENS_CATALOGUE.md (new), `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Generic lens catalogue"), `docs/USAGE.md`; API reference regenerated.
- **Integration of #564, #553, #563 and #549 (this release).**
  - **The defocus kernel is the shaped kernel or the disc** (`siteKernel ?? disc`) in the capture and the display view, so a blade-shaped kernel above 64 sites takes #564's run path. The shaped kernel's radius cap is 512 sites, refused with #564's short message (`lens.defocus: The blur circle is … across; the engine renders at most 1,025 sites …`). The run path's refusal is now a plain `lens.defocus:` message stating the 16,641-read limit, and a cat's eye above 64 sites is refused by name. The `lens.defocus` assumption gains `convolution: "runs"` whenever the applied kernel is above 64 sites.
  - **The display view's lens signal with a large disc.** #563's lens signal read only the combined kernels, so with #564 a lens view above 64 sites would have rendered without the blur. It now applies the disc by runs to each channel's in-focus signal through the lens, as the rectilinear view does. With radial distortion, vignetting and a disc of radius 179, every site matches the capture's image-space provider within 1e-9 relative, and `gpu: "auto"` falls back to the labelled CPU reference.
  - **Fixed tiles and lens profiles.** #564's fixed 64-site tiles in the image-space provider mapped lattice points up to a tile beyond the reach #548 checks, which threw with a distortion profile sized to that reach. Points beyond the reach (which feed no queried value) are now set to 0 and never mapped, and so are the lens signal's in-focus tiles. Every queried value is the same sum; only the prefix sums' rounding can change, within the run path's stated accuracy. These requests are new in 2.17.0, so no 2.16.0 bytes are involved.
  - **`resolveGenericLens` profiles reach past the corner (app report).** Its distortion and vignetting profiles stopped at the half-diagonal (21.633 mm on full frame), while the display view and the capture read slightly beyond the outer site centres (21.637 mm), so `simulateDisplayView` and `simulateCapture` refused even PV 50 1.8 with its own profiles. They now reach 1.25 of the half-diagonal (the distortion's range is widened for barrel lenses, whose image points come from farther out). The coefficients are unchanged, so every value inside the frame is too; every lens stays valid there (vignetting E > 0, distortion monotonic; tested for all 30). `createGenericLensCorrectionProfile` keeps its own 1.05.
  - **The catalogue and bokeh.** `lens.catalogue` with `blades`, `sphericalAberration` or `catsEye` is refused by name; before, the options were validated and then dropped. The catalogue's combined defocus limit of 16 sites stays as built.
  - **The app's bundle (app report).** Built against this release, the app's total JavaScript grows from 498.6 KiB (2.15.0) to 521,974 B (2.16.0) and 546,366 B gzip (2.17.0), against its 512,000 B budget. An engine bundle of exactly the app's worker imports grows the same: 311,439 → 323,428 → 347,775 B gzip over all chunks. Tree-shaking works: unused root exports such as `calculateDefocusBokehKernel` are absent. The growth is the new options' code, which `simulateCapture` and `prepareDisplayView` reach at run time. The capture worker chunk's +65 KB is mostly about 60 KB of existing modules (the display-view GPU host, text metadata, pixel limits) that the bundler now places in the worker's entry chunk instead of three chunks of their own, so they are not new bytes. `test/capture-bundle.test.ts` now checks that shape against its measured total (348,000 B gzip), so growth shows in the engine first.
  - **Tests:** `test/defocus-integration-2-17.test.ts`; the bokeh limit tests updated for the 512-site cap and the new messages.

## 2.16.0 — published November 7, 2026

A minor release of five issues: the display view's highlight count (#533), a development exposure push (#556), sharpening and diffraction correction (#552), `lens.defocus` with projection, distortion, vignetting and camera rotation (#548), and docs (#400). Captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same as 2.15.0 for every request that 2.15.0 renders and that uses none of the new options; the display view's result gains one diagnostic.
- **New:** `DisplayViewResult.diagnostics.highlight` (#533); `output.exposureCompensationStops` (#556); `output.sharpening` and `output.diffractionCorrection` (#552); out-of-focus `lens.defocus` with `lens.projection`, `lens.distortion`, `lens.vignetting` or camera rotation under a global shutter, in image space (#548).
- **Docs:** 2.15.0 recorded as published; display-view cost rows re-measured (#400).
- **Root exports:** 5 new, 1,805 → 1,810 (runtime exports 613 → 614): the type `DisplayViewHighlightDiagnostics` (#533); `NATIVE_RAW_DEVELOPMENT_EXPOSURE_RANGE_STOPS` (#556); the types `SimulateCaptureSharpening`, `SimulateCaptureDiffractionCorrection` and `SimulateCaptureDetailDiagnostics` (#552). #548 adds none.
- **Why minor:** new root exports and new opt-in options; requests that 2.15.0 refused (#548) now render. Nothing is removed or changed incompatibly, and no default changes.
- **GPU:** #533 changes `src/gpu` (the display view's Speed and Quality kernels); the hardware gates passed on an Apple M5 Pro (docs/WEBGPU_DEVICES.md, docs/RELEASE_2_16_0.md).

- **New: the display view reports its highlight count (#533, for Photivra/apps#158, #223 and #240).** `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView(...).render` return `diagnostics.highlight: { saturatedPixelCount, statistic: "expected-value" }`: the real capture's saturated output pixels (at least one clipped contributing RAW site) in the rendered region. `simulateCapture` counts these on its one noise sample as `diagnostics.highlight.saturatedPixelCount`. Until now an app could only show the count of a small quick capture.
  - **Definition:** the expected value over the noise, Σ_j w_j·(1 − Π_k (1 − p_k)). j runs over the region's real pixels, with w_j the part of each inside the region. p_k is the probability that a site pixel j's demosaic reads is at or above the white level, under the view's own site model (normal, with exact expected electrons and shot, dark, read and ADC noise). It is estimated like the expected value (exact area mean, or stratified), so it is a real number. It equals the capture's count exactly where every site is more than 8.5 σ from white. With AHD it counts the 25–29 sites the view's candidates read, not the capture's full 7 × 7 square: an approximation that can be lower at clipped corners and specks (docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "Highlight count").
  - **Additive over parts:** a display pixel's share is the same in every region, so the counts of regions that tile a view sum to the whole view's.
  - **No change to the view:** samples, expected values, noise, every other diagnostic and the assumptions are unchanged. The hash locks of `test/display-view.test.ts`, `display-view-pixel-memo.test.ts` and `display-view-speed-material.test.ts` leave the new field out and keep their recorded hashes.
  - **GPU (`src/gpu`):** the Speed and Quality kernels mark each site certainly unclipped, certainly clipped (beyond twice the reference's 8.5 σ after their error bounds) or undecided. They return a bit per real pixel that is certainly saturated, and flag a display pixel with an undecided real pixel. The host forms the count with the reference's arithmetic and takes an undecided pixel's count from a one-pixel reference render; that pixel's samples stay the GPU's. The count is therefore the reference's bit for bit and `diagnosticsExact` stays true. The run-time self-checks now also compare the count. The flag output grows from one to three words per display pixel (Speed band: 48 → 56 bytes per display pixel).
  - **Cost:** on the app request (Apple M5 Pro, Node 24.19.0, load average about 6), part medians were 38–41 ms against 2.15.0's 39 ms (Speed) and 74–79 ms against 74–78 ms (Quality). The extrapolated whole view was 0–4 % slower.
  - **Tests:** `test/display-view-highlight.test.ts`: the capture's count reproduced exactly (bilinear and AHD, three exact views); the mean of 8 noise seeds near white; additivity per display pixel and over regions; a prepared view; the GPU float32 emulation (Speed and Quality, decided and undecided pixels) against the reference bit for bit.
  - **API:** one new root export, the type `DisplayViewHighlightDiagnostics`; `DisplayViewResult.diagnostics` gains `highlight`. Nothing is removed or changed.
- **Docs (#400):**
  - 2.15.0 recorded as published (`README.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `docs/DEVELOPERS.md`, its record, this changelog).
  - App-request display-view rows re-measured on 2.15.0 (docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "A device-pixel view rendered in parts"; docs/QUICK_START.md, "Cost").
  - A stale "no GPU display-view renderer exists yet" removed from docs/USAGE.md.
- **New: development exposure push, `output.exposureCompensationStops` (#556).** Underexpose, then brighten the same RAW in development, like a raw editor's exposure slider, so dynamic range, read noise and bit depth become visible in recovered shadows (for the app's "Brighten in development" control).
  - **Model:** a linear gain 2^stops, −2 to +5 stops (whole or fractional), on each site's black-subtracted, white-normalized value (code − black)/(white − black), before demosaic, white balance, the highlight mode, colour, the tone curve and encoding. No photons are added: raised shadows show the RAW's own shot, dark and read noise and its ADC quantization, scaled by the gain. A site pushed to or past white clips at white and counts in `diagnostics.highlight.saturatedPixelCount`; `highlightMode: "reconstruct"` rolls it off. The SDR profile's `renderingExposureEv` is a different, post-clip scale and stays 0.
  - **Where:** the developed samples (`image.samples`, also with format `"dng"`), JPEG, PNG (every encoding), bilinear and AHD, `simulateIdealReference` and `simulateDisplayView` (its per-site readout moments are scaled by G and G²) honour it. The RAW codes, `result.raw` and the DNG stay the unmodified RAW; `output.whiteBalance: "auto"` is measured on the unpushed RAW.
  - **Reported** in the assumption `output.exposureCompensationStops` (`{ stops, gain, appliedTo, dng }`) when the option is given. A non-zero push is carried in `request.development` and replayed by `createNativeRawDevelopmentTask`, which gains the same optional `exposureCompensationStops`. The WebGPU accelerator's `developNativeRaw` rejects a non-zero push with `unsupported-on-backend`.
  - **Tests** (`test/simulate-capture-exposure-push.test.ts`): a +3-stop push of a 3-stop underexposure matches the normal exposure's mean within noise (0.4 % here, with about √8 times its noise) and keeps the underexposed RAW codes; after a +5-stop push of a 5-stop underexposure, a sensor with 20 e⁻ read noise is noisier than one with 1 e⁻; a pushed noise-free gradient shows 12-bit quantization plateaus and none at 14 or 16 bits; pushed highlights clip, and reconstruct rolls them off; JPEG, PNG (sRGB and Display P3), AHD, the DNG format's samples, the ideal reference and the display view are brighter by the gain, while the DNG bytes are unchanged; validation in the facade and the development task.
  - **Compatibility:** one new root export, `NATIVE_RAW_DEVELOPMENT_EXPOSURE_RANGE_STOPS` (`{ minimum: -2, maximum: 5 }`); `NativeRawDevelopmentInput` and the facade's `output` gain an optional field. Without the option (or with 0), captures, RAW codes, files, requests and assumptions are byte-identical. Docs: `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Development exposure push"), `docs/USAGE.md`, `docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md`, `docs/WEBGPU.md`; API reference regenerated.
- **New: sharpening and diffraction correction, with honest trade-offs (#552).** Two opt-in development stages of `simulateCapture`, off by default, for the app's per-correction toggles (on for the JPEG, off in the DNG).
