  - **`output.diffractionCorrection`** `{ wavelengthNm?, regularization?, maximumGain?, kernelRadiusPixels? }`, which needs `lens.diffraction`: a bounded, regularized deconvolution of the developed linear colour with the diffraction OTF of the ideal circular pupil at the capture's working f-number, H(s) = (2/π)(acos s − s√(1 − s²)), s = f/f_c, f_c = 1/(λ·N_w) (MIT OCW 2.71). The gain min(maximumGain, H(1 + α)/(H² + α)) is Wiener deconvolution with a constant noise-to-signal ratio α, built as a tapered (2R + 1)² filter with unit DC gain; its response is measured and reported. Defaults: 550 nm, α = 0.01, gain bound 4, R = the Airy third dark ring (1–16 pixels). Frequencies beyond the cut-off never reached the sensor and stay lost: the design gain there is 0, and the assumption says `recoversBeyondCutoff: false`.
  - **`output.sharpening`** `{ radiusPixels?, amount?, threshold? }`: an unsharp mask y = x + amount·(x − G_σ∗x) where |x − G_σ∗x| ≥ threshold (as in GIMP and ImageMagick), per channel on the sRGB-encoded values after the tone curve and gamut handling, before quantization. Defaults: σ = 1 pixel, amount 0.5, threshold 0. sRGB output only.
  - **Order** (the `output.developmentOrder` assumption): demosaic → colour and white balance → diffraction correction → rendering exposure and tone curve → gamut and transfer → unsharp mask → quantization. With a development exposure push (#556) the order starts with it, on the RAW.
  - **Reported** in the `output.diffractionCorrection` and `output.sharpening` assumptions (settings, defaults used, cut-off, measured filter response), in `diagnostics.detail` (ringing below black and above white; sharpened, held and clipped samples; the largest halo increase and decrease) and in `diagnostics.options`.
  - **Limits:** neither combines with `output.region`; neither is offered by `simulateDisplayView` or `simulateIdealReference`. Both hold the whole developed image, as AHD does. The DNG is never modified: with both stages a DNG request writes the same DNG bytes.
  - **Trade-off tests** (`test/lens-correction-tradeoffs.test.ts`, `test/development-detail.test.ts`; numbers in docs/DIGITAL_OPTICS_FOUNDATION.md): distortion correction stretches the corners, so a 0.25 cycle/pixel source grating lands at 0.186 cycles per output pixel in the corner (0.250 at the centre) with lower modulation; vignetting correction raises corner noise by the gain (×1.29 over the corner patch) and leaves the SNR unchanged; lateral CA correction registers the edges (to 0.005 px) but red and blue stay 1.8–2.2 px wider than green (longitudinal CA); sharpening overshoots and undershoots a step by 0.367·amount·h at σ = 1.5, as predicted; diffraction correction leaves a grating beyond the cut-off at exactly zero and restores one at 0.55·f_c only from 0.34 to 0.82 of its contrast.
  - **Compatibility:** 3 new root type exports (`SimulateCaptureSharpening`, `SimulateCaptureDiffractionCorrection`, `SimulateCaptureDetailDiagnostics`); `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_COST_OPTIONS` gains `output.diffractionCorrection` and `output.sharpening`; `diagnostics.detail` is new and present only when a stage ran. Requests without the two options give the same captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions. No version bump here.
- **`lens.defocus` out of focus with `lens.projection`, `lens.distortion`, `lens.vignetting` or camera rotation (#548, for Photivra/apps#252, Photivra/apps#230, #549 and #553).** Until now `simulateCapture` refused these combinations, because the defocus disc ran as an object-space convolution on the plane lattice the sites see, and each of these options moves the sites' scene points off that lattice (or, for vignetting, gives each point its own radiance-to-irradiance factor).
  - **Now supported, in image space:** for each spectral node (and, with rotation, each temporal midpoint, in that instant's camera frame) the sensor-plane irradiance before any PSF is formed on the sensor's site lattice through the inverse distortion, the declared projection, the rotation and the field factor (vignetting × distortion area factor), convolved with the disc, and each site query is answered with the result divided by the queried point's field factor (`src/facade/defocus-image-space-provider.ts`). It is the environment PSF quadrature with the disc as a sampled PSF, summed in another order. With `lens.diffraction`, each node's diffraction kernel convolved with the disc is the kernel.
  - **Decisions per combination:** vignetting: supported, no approximation beyond the disc (field throughput applied where the light enters, before the blur, as for any environment PSF; no commutation error). Projection and distortion: supported; the disc has the same diameter at every field point, centred on each chief ray (exact for the rectilinear distortion-free thin lens), and the `lens.defocus` assumption records `fieldVariation` bounds for the request (`distortionMaximumLocalScaleDeviation`, `projectionMaximumDiameterChangeSites`). Camera rotation with a global shutter: supported, per temporal midpoint; `rotationMaximumDiameterChangeSites` bounds the tilt of the plane under pitch and yaw (0 for roll). Still refused, each with a message that says why: rotation with `motion.shutter: "rolling"` (every row has its own instants), and a vignetting or distortion profile that does not reach the frame's corners plus the kernel radius. `lens.pupilSampling` stays refused with projection and distortion (a traced reference needs the 3D ray provider, #358). `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` refuse projection, distortion, vignetting and rotation in any focus, as before, so they share no #548 refusal and do not change.
  - **Accuracy:** RAW codes equal those of the same request with the disc passed as `lens.psf` (the per-tap environment quadrature), for each combination, all together, and with `lens.diffraction`; expected electrons agree to 1e-12 per site. The identity mapping (`lens.projection: "rectilinear"`) gives the object-space path's RAW. Against a fine-grid oracle (the disc sampled 5 times finer, 3 times with the 4 × 4 box), the lattice-sampling error is that of the rectilinear capture: on a 16 × 12 slanted edge with a 5.95-site disc, max / RMS relative difference in expected electrons 0.110 / 0.026 (point) and 0.032 / 0.006 (4 × 4 box) for the rectilinear capture, vignetting, a fisheye and radial distortion alike, and lower with rotation (0.069 / 0.016 and 0.017 / 0.003). The field-variation bounds on the app's full frame (50 mm f/2.8, focus 1 m, a 5.0-site disc): equisolid fisheye 0.43 sites, radial k1 −0.1 a local scale 0.28 off 1 at the corners, typical shake rotation 8e-4 sites.
  - **Performance** (app defaults: Speed preset, 384 × 256 whole frame at 93.75 µm with the 2 × 2 box, Siemens star, 50 mm f/2.8, subject 2 m, focus 1 m, disc 5.0 sites, R 3; Apple M5 Pro, Node.js 24.19.0, medians of 3 on a shared machine): vignetting 1.0 s in focus, 1.6 s out of focus (R 3) and 1.7 s (R 6); rotation 2.1–3.3 s in focus, 7.5–8.8 s (R 3) and 6.5 s (R 6); fisheye 11 s, 15–19 s; radial distortion 18–21 s, 19–20 s; all four together 94–97 s, 101–124 s. Out of focus without these options stays on the object-space path (0.05–0.07 s). Table in docs/QUICK_START.md ("Optics"). Without rotation and lens mapping, and with rotation, the lean executor reads the image-space values by lattice key (`InternalRadianceAccess.keyOfDirection` now takes the request time; internal).
  - **API:** `request.diffraction.execution` gains `"image-space-convolution"`; the `lens.defocus` assumption gains `execution: "image-space-convolution"`, `imageSpaceOptions` and `fieldVariation`. No new root export. Every request that 2.15.0 renders gives the same RAW codes, files and assumptions (in focus, and out of focus without these options).
  - **Tests:** `test/simulate-capture-defocus-optics.test.ts` (each combination against the environment quadrature, 1e-12 per site, diffraction, identity mapping, in focus byte-identical, bounds, refusals, regions with the Quality filters); `test/simulate-capture-defocus-render.test.ts` updated. Docs: `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Optics", "Modelling choices").

- **Integration of #556 with #552 and #548 (this release).** With `output.sharpening` or `output.diffractionCorrection` the capture develops through `developLinearSrgb` (the native development's own linear operator) instead of `createNativeRawDevelopmentTask`; the exposure push applies on both paths, on the black-subtracted, white-normalized RAW, so pushed sites clip at white before the diffraction correction. `output.developmentOrder` lists `"development exposure push (RAW)"` first when a push is given.
  - **Tests** (`test/development-detail-integration.test.ts`): a +2-stop push with an unsharp mask that changes nothing is byte-identical to the push alone (the two paths); a +2-stop push of a quarter exposure (1/32 s), sharpened and diffraction-corrected, matches the full exposure (1/8 s) column by column within noise, with the same clipped highlight (saturated counts within 5 %) and the unclipped quarter-exposure RAW; image-space `lens.defocus` (vignetting, distortion and diffraction) with each of `output.exposureCompensationStops`, `output.sharpening` and `output.diffractionCorrection`, and all three: the RAW is unchanged, the push doubles linear light per sample at +1 stop, the mask adds halos, the correction raises contrast and reports that defocus is not inverted.

## 2.15.0 — published November 7, 2026

A minor release: `lens.diffraction` at every spectral step (a fix for the app's color chart), and the defocus size limit with a pre-check as root exports (#532, #560). Captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same as 2.14.1 for every request that 2.14.1 renders.
- **Fixed:** `lens.diffraction` with `sensor.spectralStepNanometers` under 6.25 nm, for example the color chart at the recommended 5 nm.
- **New:** `DEFOCUS_DISC_KERNEL_MAXIMUM_RADIUS_SITES` and `calculateDefocusDiscKernelRadius` (#532).
- **Why minor:** 4 new root exports, and two public limits that accept more input (64 → 400 wavelengths). Nothing is removed and no default changes.

- **Fixed: `lens.diffraction` at every spectral step (owner report from the app, 2026-11-06).** The color chart with diffraction failed with "wavelengthsNm must list 1 to 64 wavelengths." The app renders the `color-patches` chart at `sensor.spectralStepNanometers: 5`, as docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md recommends for color work. That step gives the sensor quadrature 80 spectral nodes over 380–780 nm, and `lens.diffraction` builds one Airy kernel per node with `createDiffractionLimitedPsfProfile`, which took at most 64. So every step under 6.25 nm failed with diffraction, on every scene, in `simulateCapture`, `simulateDisplayView`, `prepareDisplayView` and `simulateIdealReference`.
  - **Fix, exact:** `DIFFRACTION_LIMITED_PSF_PROFILE_LIMITS.maximumWavelengths` and `LENS_PSF_CONVOLUTION_KERNELS_MAXIMUM_WAVELENGTHS` are now 400, the node count of the finest allowed step (1 nm). Each node keeps its own kernel at its own wavelength: no kernel is shared, banded or interpolated, and each equals a one-wavelength profile's kernel bit for bit. The refusal messages now state the limit ("… 1 to 400 wavelengths.").
  - **Cost made linear in the node count:** every node's site kernel used to clone and parse the whole profile, O(N²). Now one private clone, parsed once, serves a request's nodes, with the same kernels. At radius 32 that took 80 nodes from 5.1 s to 0.12 s and 400 from 124 s to 1.4 s. The Airy cell-table cache holds 800 tables (was 256), and the capture's profile cache is also limited to the kernel values it could hold before.
  - **Measured** (Apple M5 Pro, Node.js 24.19.0): a 4000 × 2666 color chart capture at 6 µm and f/16 takes 3.9 s without diffraction. With diffraction it takes 4.4 s at 20 nm, 4.3 s at 5 nm (heap 146 MiB against 128 MiB) and 4.5 s at 1 nm (218 MiB). The worst case, `kernelRadiusSamples: 32` at 1 nm on 1200 × 800, takes 35.9 s and 675 MiB. The full table is in docs/PSF_FOUNDATION.md, "Spectral nodes (2.15.0)"; also see docs/QUICK_START.md ("Optics") and docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md.
  - **Tests** (`test/diffraction-spectral-grid.test.ts`): the reproduction through all four entry points; node counts from 1 nm to 100 nm; kernels against one-wavelength profiles; every chart preset and both caller scene kinds at 1 nm and 5 nm on the tabulated and per-site paths; every option that changes how diffraction runs; the display view's GPU Quality kernels (float32 emulation) at 80 nodes; the limits.
  - **Compatibility:** no new export. Two public limits accept more input (64 → 400). Requests that 2.14.1 renders give the same values, because the kernels are bit-identical and only the cache sizes changed.

- **The defocus size limit and a pre-check are root exports (#532, for Photivra/apps#154 and Photivra/apps#157).** `lens.defocus: { model: "geometric-disc" }` refuses a disc whose kernel radius is over 64 sites (129 sites across), for example 50 mm f/1.4 focused at 1 m on a plane at 2 m with 6 µm pixels. A caller can now check a request before rendering it, instead of reading the refusal afterwards.
  - **API:** `DEFOCUS_DISC_KERNEL_MAXIMUM_RADIUS_SITES` (64) and `calculateDefocusDiscKernelRadius({ focalLengthMm, aperture, focusDistanceM, subjectDistanceM, pixelPitchMicrometers })`, which returns a `CalculationResult` (model `defocus-disc-kernel-radius` 1.0.0) with `diameterMicrometers`, `diameterSites`, `kernelRadiusSites` (0 in focus), `maximumKernelRadiusSites`, `maximumDiameterSites` (129), `withinLimit` and `excessKernelRadiusSites`. Pure: no kernel is built and nothing is rendered.
  - **Same rule as the renderer:** it calls the capture's own disc resolution (`resolveDefocusDisc`, from `calculateDefocusCircle` 1.1.0) and the same limit test as `requireDefocusDiscKernelRadius`, which `simulateCapture`, `simulateDisplayView`, `prepareDisplayView` and `simulateIdealReference` run. Pass the f-number the capture uses (in an automatic mode, `exposure.settings.fNumber`). The limit is on the disc alone; with `lens.diffraction` the combined kernel has no further limit. The other refusals of `lens.defocus` (rotation, projection, distortion, vignetting, pupil sampling, a caller PSF) are not checked.
  - **Validation:** unknown fields fail with `InvalidConfigurationError`; a missing, non-finite or non-positive input, or a distance inside the focal length, with `InvalidScientificInputError`.
  - **Tests** (`test/defocus-disc-limit.test.ts`): the limit is 64 and a disc of 129 sites is the largest within it; the closed form and the capture's resolved disc; radius 0 in focus and for a disc inside one site; at two adjacent focus distances (bisected to neighbouring doubles), the inside one renders with radius 64 and the outside one (radius 65, 1 over) is refused by `simulateCapture`, `prepareDisplayView` and `simulateIdealReference`; the same disc as a program-mode capture at its resolved f-number; input validation. `test/root-export-input-errors.test.ts` lists it as a strict calculator.
  - **Compatibility:** 4 new root exports (1,805; runtime exports 611 → 613): `DEFOCUS_DISC_KERNEL_MAXIMUM_RADIUS_SITES`, `calculateDefocusDiscKernelRadius` and the types `CalculateDefocusDiscKernelRadiusInput` and `DefocusDiscKernelRadius`. None removed or changed; captures, RAW codes, files, assumptions and error messages are unchanged. Docs: `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Optics", `lens.defocus`), `docs/USAGE.md` ("Depth of field and defocus"), `docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`, `llms.txt`; API reference regenerated.

## 2.14.1 — published November 3, 2026

A patch release of docs and legal files only. **No API, behavior or value change from 2.14.0:** `src/` differs only in `ENGINE_API_VERSION` and one source comment, root exports are unchanged (1,801), and captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same.
- **Copyright holder and trademark owner (#543, #545):** `NOTICE` names Shackelford Creative, LLC as the copyright holder (`Copyright 2026 Shackelford Creative, LLC`) and as the owner of the Photivra trade names, trademarks, logos and branding; `README.md` ("Licensing") says the same and gains a **Trademarks** paragraph for the third-party names it mentions (ColorChecker, RAL and others). `CONTRIBUTING.md` and the licence scan's allowed holder follow.
- **Licence audit fixes (#543):** `THIRD_PARTY.md` says that `NOTICE` carries castleCSF's full MIT text (the repository-only `docs/licenses/castleCSF-MIT.txt` is not packed), lists the reference colors reproduced in `docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md` with their sources, names CSS Color 4's licence as the W3C Software and Document License, and gives the pending licence reviews a dated status (#544). `docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md` attributes its reference-color column per source. The castleCSF adapter's header comment (`src/output/print-contrast-reference.ts`) now points to `NOTICE`; the emitted code is unchanged.
- **Packaged files that change:** `NOTICE`, `README.md`, `THIRD_PARTY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `CITATION.cff`, the version fields and the generated reference's version, and that one comment in `dist/output/print-contrast-reference.*`.

## 2.14.0 — published November 1, 2026

A minor release for the app's Beta: color test charts from the engine's own spectra (#537, with Photivra names, codes and reasons, #542), grey-card (incident) exposure (#538), third-stop ISO and shutter grids (#541) and a smaller package (#540). Captures, RAW codes, files and assumptions are the same as 2.13.0 for every request that uses none of the new patches, `exposure.metering: "grey-card"`, third-stop ISOs or `exposure.autoIso.isoSteps`.
- **New:** built-in Photivra color patches: the `color-patches` chart preset, the chart reflectance `{ kind: "color-patch", patchId }`, `getColorPatchSet()`, `evaluateColorPatchReflectance()`, each patch with a short code, a name and a reason (#537, #542). `exposure.greyCardDeviationStops` and `exposure.metering: "scene-average" | "grey-card"` (#538). Third-stop ISO settings on the generic tier bodies (presets 2.1.0), `exposure.autoIso.isoSteps`, and `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_SHUTTER_SECONDS` (#541).
- **Changed:** the published shader text is minified and module-level frozen tables are `/* @__PURE__ */`, so the app's bundle is 6.6 KiB gzip smaller with the same GPU results (#540).
- **Compatibility:** 21 new root exports (1,801; runtime exports 600 → 611), none removed or changed:
  - color patches, 14: 6 runtime exports (`COLOR_PATCH_SET_ID`, `COLOR_PATCH_SET_VERSION`, `COLOR_PATCH_IDS`, `COLOR_PATCH_WAVELENGTH_RANGE_NANOMETERS`, `getColorPatchSet`, `evaluateColorPatchReflectance`) and 8 types (`ColorPatch`, `ColorPatchId`, `ColorPatchGroup`, `ColorPatchSet`, `ColorPatchSpectralTerm`, `EvaluateColorPatchReflectanceInput`, `ColorPatchReflectance`, `ColorPatchesChartScene`);
  - grey card, 2: `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_METERING_MODES` and the type `SimulateCaptureMetering`;
  - third-stop grids, 5: `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_ISO_SETTINGS`, `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_SHUTTER_SECONDS`, `GENERIC_WHOLE_STOP_ISO_SETTINGS`, `GENERIC_EQUIPMENT_TIER_PRESET_VERSIONS` and the type `GenericTierPresetVersion`.
  - `ChartReflectance` and `ChartElementScene` gain a member; `CHART_SCENE_PRESETS` and `CHART_SCENE_DEFAULTS` gain `color-patches`; the capture's and the display view's `exposure` gain `greyCardDeviationStops` and `metering`; the Auto ISO report gains `isoSteps` when it is given.
  - `GENERIC_EQUIPMENT_TIER_PRESET_VERSION` is now `"2.1.0"`; `"2.0.0"` still resolves, unchanged. Captures by tier id report preset and profile version 2.1.0, and their request records list a readout regime per third-stop ISO.
  - No default changed: the meter stays `"scene-average"`, Auto ISO stays in whole stops, and a scalar reflectance is a flat grey as before.

### Added

- **Color patches designed by Photivra (docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md).** 30 reflectance spectra in six groups: a six-step flat neutral ramp from white (90.1 %) to black (3.5 %); red, yellow, green, cyan, blue and magenta, each strong and soft; four skin tones; sky blue, leaf green, brick red, sand, teal and lilac; a deep orange and a deep violet near the sRGB gamut edge. Each has a stable id, a stable short code (`PV-N1`…`PV-N6`, `PV-S1`…, `PV-T1`…, `PV-P1`…`PV-P4`, `PV-M1`…, `PV-G1`, `PV-G2`), an original Photivra name in US English ("Studio Gray 4", "Darkroom Red", "Midday Sky") and one sentence on why it is on the chart, all published in a table in docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md.
  - **Distinct from third-party colors (owner request, 2026-11-01):** no published hex equals, and no colored patch is within ΔE00 2.0 (CIEDE2000, D65) of, a CSS named color, a published ColorChecker Classic sRGB or CIELAB value, a RAL Classic approximate sRGB value or one of 10,386 brand colors from Simple Icons; neutrals, flat by definition, only avoid equal hexes (they stay within ΔE00 0.35 of the ColorChecker's neutral ramp). `scripts/check-color-patch-distinctness.mjs` downloads the pinned sources, checks their SHA-256 and discards them; no third-party color data is committed. Thirteen colors of the first design were nudged and their spectra refit: five neutrals by 0.001–0.004 in reflectance and eight colors by ΔE00 0.56–4.04 (the strong red 3.53 and the deep orange 4.04 most); Pantone was not checked (proprietary). The names do not reuse ColorChecker, CSS or RAL names.
  - **Spectra:** R(λ) = min(1, max(0, b + Σ aₖφₖ(λ))) on 360–830 nm, with Gaussian bumps exp(−½((λ − μ)/σ)²) and logistic steps 1/(1 + exp(−(λ − λ₀)/s)). Photivra fitted the parameters once to its own target colors and rounded them; the rounded values are the definition and are published in full. Every spectrum lies in [0.03, 0.901], so the clamp never acts; no fluorescence. No ColorChecker, IT8 or other commercial target's patch data, names, order, layout or measured spectra are used.
  - **API:** `{ kind: "color-patch", patchId }` is accepted wherever a chart takes a reflectance spectrum (`uniform`, `patches`, material scenes and material layers). The `color-patches` preset draws a list of patches (default: all 30, six per row) as `patches` does, with `columns`, `gapFraction` and `backgroundReflectance`; its parameters name the set and its version, and its spectra enter the scene digest. `getColorPatchSet()` returns the frozen set (ids, codes, names, groups, reasons, parameters, reference values); `evaluateColorPatchReflectance()` evaluates one spectrum (approximation, model `photivra-color-patch-spectrum` 1.0.0). Invalid ids, fields and values fail with `InvalidConfigurationError`, wavelengths outside 360–830 nm with `InvalidScientificInputError`.
  - **Reference colorimetry:** each patch's CIELAB under CIE D65 with the CIE 1931 2° observer (CIE tables, 1 nm, 360–830 nm) and its 8-bit sRGB. All 30 are inside sRGB; the gamut-edge patches' smallest linear components are 0.0105 and 0.0133. `scripts/compute-color-patch-reference.mjs` recomputes them from the CIE tables, which it downloads, checks by SHA-256 and deletes (nothing from them is stored), and with `--render` renders the chart under D65: maximum ΔE00 0.19 at 1 nm and 0.24 at 5 nm against the published values.
  - **Display view:** it evaluates the patches of chart presets with the portable exponential, so those values have the same bits in every JavaScript engine; captures use `Math.exp`.
  - **Cost:** a 600 × 500 capture of the chart costs the same as a neutral 30-patch chart (95–101 ms against 93–95 ms, one machine). The patch table adds about 9,800 B minified (about 4,100 B gzip, 3,285 B and 1,420 B of it the codes, names and reasons) to a capture-only bundle; the display view's addition is unchanged.
  - **Tests:** `test/color-patches.test.ts`:
    - the set contract, frozen records and the documented groups; codes numbered per group, names in one style and in US English, one-sentence reasons, no ColorChecker patch names;
    - the formula against the published terms on a 0.25 nm grid, and the unused clamp;
    - the neutral L* against 116·R^(1/3) − 16, the published hex against the published CIELAB, and the published hue order and skin lightness order;
    - 35 invalid reflectances, charts and inputs;
    - `color-patches` renders exactly what `patches` with the same reflectances renders, and a material scene what a uniform chart renders;
    - neutral after white balance under the 6500 K Planckian and illuminant A (ideal reference and a noisy capture);
    - hue order red, yellow, green, cyan, blue, magenta for the strong and the soft patches, lower chroma for the soft ones, skin tones by lightness;
    - CIELAB rendered under the 6500 K Planckian within ΔE00 1.35 of the published D65 values (measured 1.12, mean 0.39);
    - the display view;
    - bit-identical renders of existing charts against digests recorded with 2.13.0.
- **Incident (grey-card) exposure deviation.** `exposure.greyCardDeviationStops` = log₂(L_ref(N, t, S)/(0.18·E/π)), with L_ref = 10·N²/(0.65·t·S) the luminance that the settings expose at the CIPA DC-004-2020 standard output sensitivity reference exposure H = 10/S (§3.2; camera equation §5.5 (4), the same constants as `standardExposureDeviationStops` and `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_DEFAULT_ILLUMINANCE_LUX`), and 0.18·E/π the luminance of an 18 % Lambertian grey card under the request's illuminance E. Positive is darker (under), as for the metered deviation. It reads the light falling on the scene, as a grey card or a hand-held incident meter does, so it does not depend on the scene's reflectances: a white chart, which makes the scene meter read bright, leaves it unchanged. `null` for a `radiance-function` scene (its radiance ignores the illuminance).
  - **Exposure modes unchanged:** aperture priority, shutter priority and program still meter the scene with the body's zone meter; the new field only reports where their exposure sits against the grey card (docs/QUICK_START.md, "Exposure"). On the app's round 4 All-in-one chart (90 % white paper) at the default illuminance they expose 1.78 stops darker than the grey card.
  - **Tests:** `test/exposure-grey-card.test.ts`:
    - exactly 0 at the defaults, in the capture and the display view;
    - one stop per stop of shutter, aperture and ISO and per doubling of the illuminance (to 1e-12 stops), and the closed form at marked settings;
    - the same value for four scenes of different reflectances and for a fixed-size chart with a dark surround, while the metered deviation differs;
    - in aperture priority on a 90 % white field it exceeds the metered deviation by log₂(0.9/0.18);
    - shutter priority and program with Auto ISO follow the closed form of the settings used;
    - null for a radiance scene, 0 for a black scene at the defaults.

- **Grey-card metering (`exposure.metering`).** `"scene-average"` (default) keeps the body's 16 × 16-zone scene meter. `"grey-card"` gives every meter zone the luminance 0.18·E/π of an 18 % Lambertian grey card under the request's illuminance, so aperture priority, shutter priority and program, with a fixed ISO or Auto ISO, choose settings with `greyCardDeviationStops` = 0 whatever the scene's reflectances; only the mode's own steps and limits remain (whole-stop Auto ISO: at most half a stop, equal to the Auto ISO report's `residualStops`; the lens's f/4 or the shutter range when the light is beyond them). The result reports `exposure.metering`; with `"grey-card"` an assumption `exposure.meteringSource` gives the card's luminance. A `radiance-function` scene is refused with grey-card metering. Unknown values fail with `InvalidConfigurationError`. The scene-luminance fields keep describing the scene.
  - **Tests** (`test/exposure-grey-card.test.ts`): the default is scene-average and an explicit `"scene-average"` gives the same bytes, settings and assumptions; unknown values and a radiance scene are refused; each automatic mode lands within 1e-6 stops of 0 at two illuminances, and compensation moves it by exactly the compensation; settings do not depend on the scene's reflectances; with Auto ISO in all four modes the deviation equals the report's `residualStops` (to 1e-9), within half a stop for `iso-grid-quantization`, and an `iso-maximum` limit is reported as such; the display view and the ideal reference resolve the same settings.

    Images, RAW codes, every other exposure field, the assumptions and the scene digests are identical to 2.13.0 for manual, aperture-priority, program Auto ISO and shutter-priority captures and a display view (compared by SHA-256 between the two builds; every hash lock holds).

- **Third-stop exposure grids (owner's round 5, item 8).** The app's dials step in thirds.
  - **ISO:** the generic tier presets 2.1.0 (`GENERIC_EQUIPMENT_TIER_PRESET_VERSION`) take third-stop ISO settings, `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_ISO_SETTINGS` = 100, 125, 160, 200 … 25 600 (the rounded values of 100·2^(k/3), each within 2 %), up to each body's maximum (Consumer 6400, Prosumer 12 800, Professional 25 600), each with its own readout regime from the same gain model. Preset 2.0.0 is kept exactly (manifest pin unchanged) and still resolves; `GENERIC_EQUIPMENT_TIER_PRESET_VERSIONS` = `["2.0.0", "2.1.0"]`.
  - **Auto ISO keeps whole stops** by default, so its choices are those of 2.13; `exposure.autoIso.isoSteps: "third-stop"` lets it choose every setting (reported as `autoIso.isoSteps` when given). Auto ISO limits must lie on the grid it uses.
  - **Shutter:** `GENERIC_THIRD_STOP_SHUTTER_SECONDS`, the standard third-stop times from 1/8000 s to 30 s (55 values, the rounded values of 2^(k/3) s, each within 7 %). The bodies' shutters stay continuous and take every value as the exact time.
  - **Same output:** whole-stop requests on the tier ids give the same samples, RAW codes, files, settings and assumptions as the 2.0.0 records (`test/third-stop-settings.test.ts`, five requests on each tier), and the 22-result fingerprint is identical in every typed array. The result fields that change are the version identifiers (`provenance.cameraPresetVersion` and `exposure.resolution.capabilityProfiles.{body,lens}ProfileVersion` read 2.1.0) and the request records (`request.environmentRaw`, `request.siteInput(i)`), whose readout profile now lists a regime per third-stop ISO; `test/simulate-capture-integration.test.ts` re-pins those six request digests and none of its sample, code, flag, development or scene digests.
  - **Tests:** `test/third-stop-settings.test.ts`: the series against 100·2^(k/3) and 2^(k/3) s; both catalogs pinned; selections of both versions; ISO 125–320 expose in proportion and are refused on a 2.0.0 record; whole-stop byte identity; Auto ISO whole-stop by default and thirds on request (300 lx: 6400 → 8000); limits on the grid; every shutter time accepted as given.

### Changed

- **Smaller published package (#536).** The app was 2 KiB under its 500 KiB JavaScript budget.
  - **Shader text:** `npm run build` strips comments and indentation from the `/* wgsl */` shader literals (`scripts/minify-wgsl-dist.mjs`, `scripts/lib/wgsl-minify.mjs`), keeping every newline and every line that holds an interpolation, so the WGSL token stream, line numbers and the engine's own shader-text anchors are unchanged; the build fails if any of that changes. Tests and the WebGPU suites run the minified text (`PHOTIVRA_WGSL_MINIFY=0` for the source). docs/WEBGPU.md, "Shipped shader text".
  - **Pure annotations:** 110 module-level `Object.freeze(<literal>)` table constructors are `/* @__PURE__ */`, so bundlers drop unused tables; in-function freezes are untouched.
  - **Measured:** on the app's production build (0.50.0, Vite 8), the GPU session chunk 52,917 → 46,430 B gzip and the app's total JavaScript 500.7 → 494.1 KiB gzip; importing only `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_DEFAULT_ILLUMINANCE_LUX` 1,175 → 457 B gzip.
  - **Same output:** on an Apple M5 Pro, `npm run test:webgpu` passed all 167 tests with the minified and with the source shader text, with identical recorded values (2,911); the CPU suite and every hash lock pass with the minified text. `test/wgsl-minify.test.ts` checks every literal's tokens with an independent tokenizer, the 14 shader-text anchors, and the edge cases.

### Known

- At the default `sensor.spectralStepNanometers` (20 nm) the facade's scene-illuminant white balance is measured on 1 nm nodes while the sensor integrates on 20 nm nodes, which leaves a small neutral cast that depends on the illuminant's spectral detail: white a* 0.17, b* 0.36 under the default 6500 K Planckian, b* −0.17 under illuminant A, and a* −0.74, b* +0.95 under the CIE D65 table as a caller spectrum. With 5 nm nodes it is below 0.03. Unchanged in this release (changing it would change existing captures); docs/COLOR_PATCHES.md recommends 5 nm for color work.

## 2.13.0 — published October 31, 2026

A minor release for the app's Beta camera: Auto ISO with photographer-set limits and an automatic slowest shutter, the illuminance that gives the standard exposure, and root exports for adding text metadata to a file the caller encoded. Captures, RAW codes and files are the same as 2.12.0 for the same request; Quality (AHD) display views change where the scene decides AHD's choice, closer to the capture (#457).
- **New:** `estimateAutoIsoSlowestShutter()` and `exposure.autoIso.slowestPreferredShutterSeconds: "auto"` (#504); `autoIsoLimits` on every Auto ISO resolver and `exposure.autoIso.limits` (#505); `exposure.standardExposureIlluminanceLux`; `addJpegTextMetadata` and `addPngTextMetadata` (#388).
- **Fixed:** the Quality (AHD) display view follows AHD's direction choice (#457).
- **Faster, same output:** diffraction captures (#419); the print JPEG task (#417).
- **Compatibility:** 13 new root exports (1,780), none removed or changed; new optional options and result fields only; no default changed. `ExposureResolutionConstraint` gains two members, `"user-iso-maximum"` and `"user-iso-minimum"`, reported only when the new limits are given.

### Added

- **Auto ISO: an automatic slowest shutter from the lens and the hand's shake, with a faster/slower bias (#504).** New `estimateAutoIsoSlowestShutter()` (approximation, model `camera-shake-blur-budget-slowest-shutter` 1.0.0). It gives the exposure time at which `estimateCameraShakeBlur()`'s stabilized on-axis blur, v·hypot(tan(r·ω_yaw·t), tan(r·ω_pitch·t)) with r = 2^−stops, equals a blur budget (in pixels of a pitch, or a fraction of the image diagonal). Then it applies a whole-stop bias `fasterByStops` (−2 to +2: derived · 2^−bias) and, when `shutterCapability` is given, snaps to the body's shutter grid without ever lengthening the time. One axis uses the closed form t = atan(budget/v)/(r·|ω|); two axes use deterministic bisection. The 1/focal-length reciprocal rule is a rule of thumb and is not used. Makers' manuals for the feature: Sony ISO AUTO Min. SS, Fujifilm ISO Auto setting, Nikon D850 Auto ISO control.
  - **Hand shake, a labelled generic default:** `GENERIC_HANDHELD_ANGULAR_VELOCITY_RAD_PER_SEC` = 4.39 °/s (0.0766 rad/s) on yaw. It is the linear camera-motion rate for an average user holding a 100 g handset with two hands, in F. Xiao, J. Farrell, P. Catrysse, B. Wandell, "Mobile imaging: the big challenge of the small pixel", Proc. SPIE 7250 (2009), §2 (free author copy at web.stanford.edu/~wandell). It comes from the gyroscope measurements of Xiao et al., Proc. SPIE 6502 (2007). It describes a phone, not any camera or photographer, so the estimator takes the profile as an explicit input; the default is only the facade's.
  - **Program + Auto ISO:** `resolveProgramAutoExposureMode` takes an optional `isoControl.slowestPreferredShutter: { slowestPreferredShutterSeconds, afterMaximumIso }` (`ProgramSlowestPreferredShutterPolicy`). The line's shutter is held at or faster than the threshold, with the line's aperture, before the ISO rises. At the Auto ISO maximum (the body's or #505's `autoIsoLimits`), `allow-slower-shutter` lengthens the shutter toward the target but never past the line's own shutter, and `hold-preferred-shutter` keeps it. The result adds `slowestPreferredShutter: { …, applied, afterMaximumIsoFallbackUsed }` only when the option is given. Full Auto does not take it.
  - **`simulateCapture` and the display view:** `exposure.autoIso.slowestPreferredShutterSeconds` accepts `"auto"` in aperture priority and is now optional in program (a number or `"auto"`, with `afterMaximumIso`). `exposure.autoIso.automaticSlowestShutter` sets `fasterByStops` (default 0), `blurBudgetPixels` (default 1), `angularVelocityRadPerSec` (default the generic hand) and `stabilizationStopsEquivalent` (default 0); each default is a `default` assumption, and the derivation is a `model` assumption. The Auto ISO report gains `automaticSlowestShutter` (derived, biased and used times, budget, blur at the used time in pixels) and, in program, `slowestPreferredShutterApplied`.
  - **Unchanged without the options:** every hash lock holds. A numeric slowest shutter in aperture priority and program without the option give the same results and reports as before.
  - **Tests:** `test/auto-iso-slowest-shutter.test.ts`:
    - the closed form, and the shake model's blur at the derived time equals the budget (one and two axes);
    - nine focal lengths give strictly faster thresholds, with 400 mm 8× faster than 50 mm;
    - the thin-lens projection at a focus distance;
    - stabilization lengthens by exactly 2^stops;
    - the bias moves by exact whole stops;
    - the diagonal-fraction budget;
    - the discrete-grid snap and its clamps;
    - no NaN or Infinity across a sweep;
    - 15 invalid inputs fail with domain errors.

    `test/auto-iso-limits.test.ts` adds the program hold: applied and not applied, both after-maximum policies, the fallback stopping at the line's shutter, and invalid holds. `test/simulate-capture-auto-iso.test.ts` adds the facade:
    - aperture priority with `"auto"`;
    - equality with a direct estimator call;
    - focal length and bias;
    - program with a number and with `"auto"`;
    - the display view report;
    - 11 invalid options fail.
  - New root exports (1,780): `estimateAutoIsoSlowestShutter`, `GENERIC_HANDHELD_ANGULAR_VELOCITY_RAD_PER_SEC`, `AUTO_ISO_SLOWEST_SHUTTER_MODEL_VERSION`, `AUTO_ISO_SLOWEST_SHUTTER_MAXIMUM_BIAS_STOPS`, and the types `EstimateAutoIsoSlowestShutterInput`, `AutoIsoSlowestShutterBlurBudget`, `AutoIsoSlowestShutterEstimate`, `ProgramSlowestPreferredShutterPolicy`, `SimulateCaptureAutomaticSlowestShutterOptions` and `SimulateCaptureAutomaticSlowestShutterReport`. Docs: `docs/USAGE.md` ("Automatic slowest shutter (#504)", "Program Auto with Auto ISO: a slowest preferred shutter (#504)"), `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Auto ISO") and `docs/STABILIZATION.md`.
- **Auto ISO: photographer-set limits, separate from the body's capability (#505).** A new optional policy input `autoIsoLimits: { maximum, minimum? }` (type `AutoIsoLimits`) on every Auto ISO entry point: `resolveManualExposureMode` (`isoControl.autoIsoLimits`), `resolveAperturePriorityAutoIsoExposureMode` (`policy.autoIsoLimits`), `resolveShutterPriorityExposureMode` (`isoControl.policy.autoIsoLimits`), `resolveProgramAutoExposureMode` (`isoControl.autoIsoLimits`) and `resolveFullAutoExposureMode` (`policy.autoIsoLimits`); and `exposure.autoIso.limits` in `simulateCapture`, the display view and the ideal reference, in every mode. Sources, makers' manuals: Nikon D850 Auto ISO control (maximum sensitivity), Fujifilm X-Pro2 ISO Auto setting (default and maximum sensitivity), Panasonic GH5 II (upper and lower Auto ISO limits).
  - **Policy, not capability:** the body's `capabilities.iso.autoIsoMinimum`/`autoIsoMaximum` (else its ISO range) still say what the body allows; the limits narrow that range. Both must be selectable ISOs inside it, `minimum` at most `maximum` (default the body's minimum); otherwise the resolvers fail with `InvalidScientificInputError` (a malformed record with `InvalidConfigurationError`) and `simulateCapture` with `InvalidConfigurationError`. Nothing is clipped. The policies' lowest-selectable-ISO baseline starts at the user's minimum.
  - **Reporting:** `ExposureResolutionConstraint` gains `"user-iso-maximum"` and `"user-iso-minimum"`, reported when the ISO is held at a limit strictly inside the body's range (a limit equal to the body's bound reports the body's `"iso-maximum"`/`"iso-minimum"`). `SimulateCaptureAutoIsoReport` gains the optional `limits: { minimum, maximum }`, the range used; `autoIsoMinimum`/`autoIsoMaximum` stay the body's range. The `exposure.autoIso` assumption records the limits when given.
  - **Unchanged without the input:** no result, sample or hash changes when `autoIsoLimits` / `exposure.autoIso.limits` is absent; every hash lock holds. `exposure.autoIso` is now accepted with `iso: "auto"` in every mode, but `slowestPreferredShutterSeconds` and `afterMaximumIso` stay aperture priority only. A separate flash cap is not included (it belongs with flash-aware exposure).
  - **Tests:** `test/auto-iso-limits.test.ts` (each of the five resolvers stops at the user maximum with `user-iso-maximum` and the exact residual; a maximum equal to the body's reports `iso-maximum` and changes nothing; the user minimum raises the baseline and reports `user-iso-minimum`; aperture priority's `allow-slower-shutter` and `hold-preferred-shutter` give the same settings and residual against a user cap of 800 as against a body capped at 800; limits equal to the body's range give results equal to no limits; out-of-range, off-grid, unordered, non-finite, missing and malformed limits fail in every resolver) and `test/simulate-capture-auto-iso.test.ts` (the facade in manual, aperture priority, shutter priority and program; a capped capture byte-identical to that manual ISO; the display view and ideal reference report the same limits; ten invalid options fail).
  - New root export: the type `AutoIsoLimits` (1,770 root symbols). Docs: `docs/USAGE.md` ("Photographer-set Auto ISO limits (#505)", "Priority modes with Auto ISO") and `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Auto ISO").
- **The illuminance at which the settings give the standard exposure.** `simulateCapture`'s and the display view's `exposure` gain `standardExposureIlluminanceLux`, next to `standardExposureDeviationStops`: E·L_ref(N, t, S)/L = E·2^Δ, with E the request's `illuminanceLux`, L the metered scene luminance and L_ref = 10·N²/(0.65·t·S) (CIPA DC-004-2020, as for Δ). A chart, reflectance-function or material scene's radiance is reflectance × k(E)·S(λ)/π with k proportional to E, so its metered luminance is proportional to E and the same request at this illuminance meters at Δ = 0. It is `null` for a black scene (L = 0) and for a `radiance-function` scene, whose radiance does not follow the illuminance; it may exceed the 1e7 lux that `illuminanceLux` accepts. No sample, RAW code or other result changes; every hash lock holds.
  - Tests (`test/exposure-standard-illuminance.test.ts`): re-running at the reported illuminance gives |Δ| < 1e-9 stops (≈ 2e-15 measured; the 16 × 16-zone, 5 nm metering quadrature is the same at both illuminances and cancels) for the default request at f/11 (40 612 lux) and a 3200 K Siemens star at 5000 lux (10 557 lux); with the defaults it is the default illuminance to 1e-4 (the quadrature's ≈ 1.2 × 10⁻⁵ stop); aperture priority equals manual at the settings it resolved, shutter priority and program are E·2^Δ, the display view equals the capture; `null` for a black and a radiance scene. `test/simulate-capture-auto-iso.test.ts` expects the new field in the display view's exposure.
- **Display-view bundle size, measured and guarded (#383).** Tests and documentation only; no `src/` change, so no result, sample or RAW code changes and every hash lock holds. Sizes with rolldown 1.2.9 (Vite 8's bundler), minified, gzip level 9, from 2.11.1's `dist`, in docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "Bundle size":
  - **The display view adds 42,228 B minified and 16,888 B gzip** to a `simulateCapture` + `simulateIdealReference` bundle (846,928 B / 229,007 B gzip). #383's target of 40 % less is not met: the addition is mostly the renderer itself (55 % of the rendered size), the result's assumption texts and the portable functions, and no smaller form was found that keeps every result's bytes and the measured render times. `test/capture-bundle.test.ts` keeps the addition within a budget about 2 % above the measured size: 43,100 B minified and 17,250 B gzip on 2.11.1's display view, raised to 46,500 B and 18,550 B for #457's AHD choice (#527), which added 3,307 B minified and 1,294 B gzip (the test's build from `src/` measures 45,546 B / 18,186 B gzip; all of the growth is `facade/display-view.ts`, rendered 55,908 → 64,855 B, with no new import and no `src/gpu` module in the entry).
  - **The GPU display view stays out of a split bundle's first load:** a bundle of `simulateCapture`, `simulateDisplayView` and `prepareDisplayView` has no `src/gpu` module in its entry chunk (892,566 B / 247,193 B gzip); the GPU session, the renderers and their WGSL (15 `src/gpu` modules, 52,446 B gzip) are a chunk `prepareDisplayView` imports only for `gpu: "auto"` or `"require"`. The new test fails if they reach the entry. A build that cannot split inlines them (302,217 B gzip as one `iife` file): Vite's default `iife` worker format is how the GPU code reached the Photivra app's capture worker in 2.11; with `worker: { format: "es" }` its worker measured 258,618 B gzip instead of 317,120 B. docs/USAGE.md notes this under "Rendering a prepared view on the GPU".
- **`addJpegTextMetadata` and `addPngTextMetadata` are root exports (#388).** A caller that encodes a JPEG or PNG itself (for example a full-size file joined from `output.region` parts with `composePrintJpegStrips` or `serializePngImage`) adds the caller's text with the functions `output.metadata` already calls, so the bytes are the same as a whole-frame `simulateCapture` with `output.metadata`. This lets Photivra/apps delete its adapted copy (`src/export/textMetadata.ts`). No file, sample or RAW code changes; every hash lock holds.
  - **Validation:** the bytes must be a `Uint8Array`; the text is checked by `output.metadata`'s rules (messages name `addJpegTextMetadata metadata` or `addPngTextMetadata metadata`). The JPEG writer now also rejects, with `InvalidConfigurationError`, a marker without a length before SOS (SOI, EOI, RSTm, TEM, fill bytes), a segment length below 2, an XMP APP1 segment after a non-APPn segment (before, it would have duplicated the bytes in between), and an XMP packet that is not UTF-8 (before, a raw `TypeError`). The engine's own JPEGs have none of these.
  - **Tests** (`test/output-text-metadata.test.ts`): for three files (8-bit JPEG, 8-bit PNG, 16-bit Display P3 PNG) and four texts (the app's export text, the app encoder test's `{ description: "x" }`, and this file's Latin-1 and non-Latin vectors), the exported functions give the bytes of `simulateCapture` with `output.metadata`, locked by SHA-256 digests that were recorded three ways and agreed: the app's copy run unmodified on the same inputs, `output.metadata` at origin/main `50dfe3e`, and the exports; the same for a JPEG without an XMP packet (against the app's copy) and the ideal-reference PNG; plus sixteen invalid inputs.
  - **Naming:** `add*` is a new verb family in `docs/API_STYLE.md` (a copy of an encoded file with caller data inserted, every other byte kept), with `test/api-style-compatibility.test.ts` updated; the two functions are classified in `test/root-export-input-errors.test.ts` (raw bytes and the text).
  - New root exports: `addJpegTextMetadata` and `addPngTextMetadata` (1,769). Docs: `docs/USAGE.md` ("Text metadata on a file you encoded"), `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Text metadata (2.4)"); API reference regenerated.

### Changed

- **Tests and CI: three hash locks are x64 locks, with recorded arm64 hashes (#508).** No engine output, package content or x64 hash lock changes. The display view's "keeps its bytes" locks, the pixel-memo locks and the Airy cell construction locks were recorded on x64; on arm64 (Apple silicon and Linux arm64, Node 24.19.0 and 24.22.1) 13 of their 17 digests differ, because V8's `Math` `sin`, `cos`, `asin`, `atan2` and `log` return results one ulp apart on the two architectures for some arguments (first a `Math.cos` in the β = 12.5 Airy cells and `Math.log(14)` in the display view; neither architecture is the more accurate). On x64 the tests assert the locks exactly as before; on arm64 (`process.arch === "arm64"`) each differing lock is asserted against its own recorded arm64 hash (`test/helpers/architecture-hash-locks.ts`), so arm64 is locked too, and the whole suite passes on Apple silicon. The display-view test no longer claims "the same on every JavaScript engine". The `hash locks (macos-latest)` job's step for these three files no longer has `continue-on-error`, so a failure there fails the step instead of showing as passed. Docs: `docs/NUMERICAL_CORRECTNESS.md` ("Test hash locks and platforms", which also corrects the macOS job to eight test files) and `docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md` ("Same bytes in every engine").

### Fixed

- **Display view, AHD (Quality): the expected value follows AHD's choice where the scene decides it (#457).** The view averaged AHD's two directional candidates at every real pixel. Where structure finer than a few sites decides AHD's homogeneity choice (a Siemens star's centre, a sharp edge), the capture takes one candidate, so at device-pixel widths the view's colour at the star's centre was 5.1 codes RMS and up to 34 codes from the capture (24 MP at 2028 wide; limit 2). The view now runs AHD's own steps on the noise-free mosaic (the expected sites, clipped at white, reflect-101 at the raster edges, as `createAhdDemosaicTask`) and takes the chosen candidate's expected value; a tie, which is every flat neighbourhood, keeps the average, and the noise terms are unchanged. docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md, "AHD's choice where the scene decides it".
  - **Measured** (seven reference seeds, limits unchanged): set F star centre 5.13 → 0.78 codes RMS (24 MP, 2028 wide) and 3.60 → 1.02 (100 MP, 4000 wide); AHD alone 19.3 → 2.84 against the seven-reference mean, where one reference is 3.08 from it. On the default matrix 50 of 75 Quality cases change and none leaves a limit; star-centre RMS falls (for example 1.64 → 0.82 codes, 24 MP at 800 wide), case 17's false-colour difference +0.41 → +0.01 and case 92's +0.48 → +0.41. No Speed case and no uniform patch changes, so the Quality noise limit (#379) is unchanged.
  - **The view's direction is the task's:** equal to `createAhdDemosaicTask`'s direction map at every pixel of a random and a star mosaic, borders included (`test/display-view-ahd-choice.test.ts`, with the set F star case against seven captures as a regression test).
  - **Bytes:** Quality (AHD) display views change wherever the two candidates differ; Speed views and the views of uniform scenes keep their bytes. Three hash locks were re-recorded on Linux x64, each closer to the capture against 16 reference captures: `test/display-view.test.ts` (AHD dead leaves, Reinhard: 0.37 → 0.16 codes RMS), `test/display-view-pixel-memo.test.ts` (AHD star: 1.51 → 0.61) and `test/display-view-speed-material.test.ts` (AHD patch edge at ISO 3200: 7.56 → 3.88; its `sceneLookups` diagnostic rises 26 400 → 28 252 because the choice reads 5 sites around each pixel). The other locks hold. Captures are unchanged.
  - **Cost:** +12–15 % on the app's Quality request at 2028 × 1352 (whole view extrapolated 23.4–24.4 s → 26.9–28.0 s, Node 24.19.0, Apple M5 Pro), +12–21 % for whole Quality views on the stratified estimator.
  - **Bundle:** the display view's addition to a capture bundle grows by 3,307 B minified and 1,294 B gzip (42,239 → 45,546 B, 16,888 → 18,186 B gzip; rolldown 1.2.9, `test/capture-bundle.test.ts`'s build), all in `facade/display-view.ts`; #383's budget is raised for it.
  - **GPU (Quality):** the kernel still renders the candidates' average; it now flags (`ahdChoice`) and the host recomputes with the CPU reference every display pixel where the choice could move it by more than 2⁻¹⁰ of its noise σ, recomputing runs of flagged pixels as one region. GPU renders keep agreeing with the CPU reference within their bounds, but busy scenes recompute a large share on the CPU (3–75 % of the emulation fixtures' pixels; 21.6 % of a 24 MP checkerboard band at 2028 × 1352). Computing the choice in the pixel pass is the follow-up. docs/WEBGPU.md, "Display view (Quality) on the GPU".

### Faster, same output

- **`simulateCapture` with `lens.diffraction`: the object-space convolution computes only each band's own values (#419).**
  - **What changed:** each 32-row band convolved its whole padded grid, (rows + 2R) × (columns + 2R), and kept the centre (about 3× the rows at R = 32, and more on narrow rasters). It now gathers only the interior (internal `createIrradianceConvolutionInteriorRunner`), with the same validation and messages as before. The scene samples per band are unchanged. Defocus (#492) already did this.
  - **Same bytes:** each interior value is the same products added in the same order (v outer, u inner, every tap), so it is `calculateIrradianceConvolution`'s value bit for bit. Checked with `Object.is` on 2,354,304 provider values against main's provider (R 1–32, material and radiance-function scenes, three offset sets, four windows, three bands), on new unit tests of the interior runner, and on twelve captures whose samples, RAW codes and `providerEvaluationCount` are identical (SHA-256) to main's. Every hash lock holds.
  - **Measured** (Node 24.19.0, one thread, an 18-core Mac shared with other jobs, load 6–43, five rounds alternating fresh processes of the two builds; medians): `diagnostics.timingsMs.diffraction` on a function scene with the Quality options (diffraction, four-spot, AHD) at 96 × 72 went 0.86 → 0.16 s at 1.4 µm f/8 (R 15), 4.9 → 0.54 s at f/16 (R 29) and 6.3 → 0.62 s at 1 µm f/16 (R 32), total 7.2 → 1.8 s; at 192 × 144 and R 32, 17.0 → 2.1 s (total 19.6 → 5.2 s). The tabulated capture's four verification sites (a 2 × 2 window) went 0.41 → 0.004 s at 1.4 µm f/16 (total 0.85 → 0.50 s at 96 × 72 and 1.05 → 0.76 s at 384 × 288). At small R nothing slows down: 6 µm f/8 and the 24 MP whole-frame view are within ±5 % over eight rounds.
  - **Not done:** a row ring sharing the 2R overlap samples between bands. Sampling is about 6 % of the band time at R 29–32 after this change (measured), so the ring would save at most about 4 % of it, for up to 2R rows × columns × nodes × lattices of doubles (about 42 MB per lattice on a 4000-site row).
- **Print JPEG task: each block's samples in one reused `Uint8Array`, without the per-sample check (#417).** `createPrintJpegTask` built every 8 × 8 block's 192 samples as a new `number[]` by `push`, and the encoder then checked each entry with `Number.isInteger` and the 0–255 bounds. Both paths (1:1 and area average) now write the samples into one reused `Uint8Array(192)`, and the internal encoder's `writeRgbBlock` skips the entry scan for a `Uint8Array`, whose entries are integers 0–255 by type; it still checks the length. The strip path (`createPrintJpegStrip`, the app's workers) already coded from a `Uint8Array` and is unchanged. #416 (2.8.1) had already replaced the 1:1 accumulation with a 256-entry table; the DCT and entropy coding are untouched.
  - **Same bytes:** the files' SHA-256 equals main's (50dfe3e) for nine plans: 6000 × 4000 noise and smooth, 3000 × 2000 noise, 1001 × 757 noise, 9 × 7, 256 × 32, 1543 × 1 at 1:1, and 3000 × 2000 → 1500 × 1000 and 1001 × 757 → 270 × 205 area averages. Every JPEG hash lock holds. New test: a reused `Uint8Array` block codes to the same bytes as arrays, and a 191- or 193-sample one is refused.
  - **Measured** (Node 24.19.0, one thread, Apple M5 Pro, with six other agents running on the machine, so absolute times are noisy; builds alternated, 3 rounds × 3 runs, median of 9): 24 MP noise at 1:1, q = 2: 3.06 → 2.90 s (×0.95); 24 MP smooth at 1:1, q = 1: 1.46 → 1.32 s (×0.91); 6 MP → 1.5 MP area average: 0.363 → 0.349 s (×0.96). The saving is below #417's profiled ceiling of about 27 %.

## 2.12.0 — published October 31, 2026

A minor release for the app's Beta camera: Auto ISO through the one-call API, and the deviation from the standard exposure for an exposure meter. With `iso` absent or a number, samples, RAW codes and files are the same as 2.11.1; the exposure result gains `isoControl` and `standardExposureDeviationStops`.
- **New:** `exposure.iso: "auto"` in every exposure mode, with `exposure.autoIso` in aperture priority and an Auto ISO report in the result (#516, #518); `exposure.standardExposureDeviationStops` (#515, #517).
- **Compatibility:** two new root type exports (1,767), none removed; `exposure.iso` widens to `number | "auto"`; new result fields only.

### Added

- **`simulateCapture` and the display view: Auto ISO in every exposure mode (#516).** `exposure.iso: "auto"` hands the ISO to the existing exposure-mode resolvers' Auto ISO branches, within the body's Auto ISO range, in `manual`, `aperture-priority`, `shutter-priority` and `program`; the default stays ISO 100. With `iso` absent or a number nothing changes: every hash lock holds, and the only result change is the new `exposure.isoControl: "manual"`.
  - **Per mode:** manual keeps the aperture and shutter (`resolveManualExposureMode`, automatic ISO); aperture priority uses `resolveAperturePriorityAutoIsoExposureMode` with the new `exposure.autoIso: { slowestPreferredShutterSeconds, afterMaximumIso? }` (the slowest preferred shutter is required, within the body's shutter range; `afterMaximumIso` is `"allow-slower-shutter"`, the default, or `"hold-preferred-shutter"`); shutter priority uses the `minimum-iso-aperture-first` policy; program uses the program line at the lowest ISO and raises the ISO beyond the line's end. Every policy starts at the lowest selectable Auto ISO and snaps to the nearest selectable ISO in stops (the lower on a tie). The automatic slowest shutter (#504) and photographer-set limits (#505) are not part of this change.
  - **Result:** `exposure.settings.iso` is the ISO used; `exposure.isoControl` is `"automatic"`; `exposure.autoIso` (`SimulateCaptureAutoIsoReport`) gives `idealIsoBeforeConstraints`, `autoIsoMinimum`, `autoIsoMaximum`, `clamped`, the resolver's `limitingConstraint` (for example `"iso-maximum"`) and `residualStops`, and in aperture priority the policy, the slowest preferred shutter and `afterMaximumIsoFallbackUsed`. The display view (`simulateDisplayView`, `prepareDisplayView`) and the ideal reference report the same fields; a new `exposure.autoIso` assumption records the policy.
  - **Validation:** a string other than `"auto"`, `exposure.autoIso` outside aperture priority with Auto ISO, a missing, non-finite or out-of-range slowest preferred shutter, an unknown `afterMaximumIso` or key, and a body without Auto ISO fail with `InvalidConfigurationError`.
  - **Tests** (`test/simulate-capture-auto-iso.test.ts`): each mode with `"auto"` (the default scene at f/8, 1/125 s gives ISO 100; three stops darker gives ISO 800, byte-identical to a manual ISO 800 capture in manual and aperture priority; compensation; the minimum and maximum with their limiting constraints; both `afterMaximumIso` policies; shutter priority past the widest aperture; program past the line's end); a capture, its display view (direct and prepared), its ideal reference and its region parts give the same exposure and report, and the parts stitch to the capture's bytes; fourteen invalid options fail with a domain error in the capture and the prepared view.
  - New root exports: the types `SimulateCaptureAutoIsoAfterMaximum` and `SimulateCaptureAutoIsoReport` (1,767). `SimulateCaptureOptions.exposure.iso` widens to `number | "auto"` and gains `autoIso`; the capture, display-view and ideal-reference `exposure` gain `isoControl` and the optional `autoIso`. Docs: `docs/QUICK_START.md` ("Inputs and defaults", "Auto ISO") and `docs/USAGE.md`.
- **The deviation from the standard exposure, in stops (#515, for Photivra/apps#124).** `simulateCapture`'s and the display view's `exposure` gain `standardExposureDeviationStops`: Δ = log₂(N²/t) − log₂(0.65·L·S/10), from the CIPA DC-004-2020 relations the facade already meters with (S = 10/H, §3.2; H = 0.65·L·t/N², §5.5 (4)), with the settings used and `meteredSceneLuminanceCdPerSquareMeter`; no new metering. Positive is darker than the standard exposure (under), negative brighter (over); in an automatic mode it is minus the compensation plus any step or limit the resolver hit. It is `null` when the metered luminance is 0 (a black scene, which manual mode captures), never infinite. No other result, sample or RAW code changes; every hash lock holds.
  - Tests: `test/exposure-deviation.test.ts`: 0 with the defaults (|Δ| ≈ 1.2 × 10⁻⁵ from the metering quadrature), +0.919 at f/11, +1.000 at f/8√2, −1.059 at 1/60 s, −1.000 at ISO 200, −⅔ in aperture priority with +⅔ stop, the 30 s limit at 0.01 lux (+9.16), the display view's value equal to the capture's, and `null` for a black scene.

## 2.11.1 — published October 31, 2026

A patch release that publishes 2.11.0's engine to npm. The owner tagged `v2.11.0` on `6bef763` and published its GitHub release on October 28, 2026, but its npm publish run (40636849817) was cancelled at the verify job's 25-minute limit while the single-job coverage gate was still running, so `@photivra/engine@2.11.0` was never published; npm `latest` is 2.10.0. A published tag runs the publish workflow of its own commit, so 2.11.0 cannot be published again; 2.11.1 carries the fixed workflow.
- **No API, behaviour or value change from 2.11.0:** `src/` differs only in `ENGINE_API_VERSION`; the packed files differ only in the version fields and the release documentation (docs/RELEASE_2_11_1.md, "Same package").
- **Publish workflow (#511):** coverage runs as four time-balanced shards in parallel with `verify`; `coverage gate` merges them and enforces the thresholds on the whole suite; `publish` needs both. A shard skips only on ci.yml's coverage proof of the exact tree and Node.js release.
- **For users of 2.11.0's GitHub release:** the features and compatibility notes are those of 2.11.0 below.

## 2.11.0 — tagged October 28, 2026; not published to npm (see 2.11.1)

A minor release: the GPU preview. `prepareDisplayView(request, { gpu, gpuDevice })` renders the display view on WebGPU (the Speed and Quality kernels, every engine chart and declared `material-layers` scene, and `lens.defocus`) and labels which backend ran; `simulateCapture` and the display view render `lens.defocus` out of focus; one switch turns the engine's GPU use off for a realm. With the default `gpu: "off"` and without `lens.defocus`, samples, RAW codes and files are the same as 2.10.0; display views gain `diagnostics.fast.execution`.
- **New:** `setWebGpuEnabled`/`getWebGpuEnabled` and the accelerator's `webgpu` boolean (#474); `prepareDisplayView(request, { gpu, gpuDevice })`, `dispose()` and `diagnostics.fast.execution` (#481); the GPU display view in Speed (#478) and Quality (#479), with composite charts and `material-layers` scenes (#477, #491) and defocus (#492); `lens.defocus: { model: "geometric-disc" }` in captures and display views (#492, an approximation).
- **GPU accuracy** (float32 emulation and SwiftShader, a software adapter; not hardware): every component inside its a-priori bound on every fixture; ΔE2000 at most 4.7 × 10⁻⁵ in Speed, 4.1 × 10⁻⁴ in Quality and 1.7 × 10⁻⁴ out of focus; samples differ from the CPU's by at most one code; the noise draw is bit-identical to the CPU's. The WebGPU kernels' margins now follow the WGSL accuracy table (#471), and the Speed variance bound names the output quantization term (#496).
- **Not measured:** GPU timing on hardware (TBD, #475). The owner's Apple M5 Pro runs of `npm run accept:gpu:hardware` and `npm run test:webgpu:hardware` are the release gate for the GPU preview.
- **Compatibility:** twenty-three new root exports (1,765), none removed; new optional request fields and options; new members of result and reason unions (`ImageKernelFallbackReason` `webgpu-disabled`, `ImageKernelFailureReason` `conformance-failed`, `lens.defocus` in `SIMULATE_CAPTURE_COST_OPTIONS`); `PreparedDisplayView` gains `dispose()` and `ImageKernelAccelerator` gains `adapter`.

### Added

- **Display view: `lens.defocus` on the GPU (#492, step 2c).** The engine's GPU display-view renderer (`createDisplayViewGpuRenderer`) now renders defocused views for Speed and Quality requests: its `supports()` accepts `DisplayViewGpuRequest.defocus`. No result changes with the default `gpu: "off"`, no CPU byte changes (every display-view hash lock holds), and in focus every GPU result is bit-identical to before (the emulation's samples, expected values, noise and bounds hashed on main and on this change for seven in-focus fixtures). The CPU display view's per-node kernels (the disc, or each node's diffraction kernel convolved with it, up to the caps of radius 64 and 96) are gathered in the Quality site pass:
  - **Speed takes the Quality passes.** Speed has no gather; out of focus the expected signal has no query points (it reads the lattice formula), so the Speed kernel's query arithmetic does not apply. A defocused Speed request runs the Quality lattice and site passes, whose lattice and gather already exist, and the Quality pixel pass with one candidate, its own bilinear demosaic, and the same host finishing. The disc is the same at every node, and the table already folds the nodes, so the gather runs once per site. In-focus Speed stays on the Speed kernel, unchanged.
  - **Factored table.** A neutral material's value is its reflectance times the white's combined kernel, so the defocus pass uploads one neutral row and one row per spectral material (3·(1 + S)·T values) and multiplies by the material's reflectance on the device. #491's composite scene has 4 143 neutral materials; a table per material would need 134 MB at radius 12 and 7 GB at radius 94.
  - **Blocked sum.** Each group's taps are a dense cw × ch rectangle, so a site sums rows, then groups, then the total (`DISPLAY_VIEW_QUALITY_SITES_BLOCKED_WGSL`, no tap records). The site's relative bound is (cw_max + ch_max + G + 3)·E: 1.5 × 10⁻⁶ at radius 1 and 4.6 × 10⁻⁵ at radius 94 (144 400 taps per site), against (T + 3)·E = 1.7 % that the tap-list sum would carry there. The bound therefore does not widen with the tap count: at radius 94 it is below the in-focus 400-tap term (4.8 × 10⁻⁵). Each term's float32 roundings (its row value and its reflectance, 2⁻²⁴ each, and the product) are inside the + 3, and terms below the float32 normal range that a device may flush add their mass.
  - **Lattice resolution.** With a wide disc nearly every site reads some lattice point the material module flags as uncertain, and that point's spread over every material flagged 95–100 % of the composite's pixels for CPU recomputation. In defocused renders the host now reads back the lattice and resolves each flagged point with the reference's own `materialAt` at the reference's lattice point, so no site carries material uncertainty and no fixture pixel is flagged (62–361 points resolved per block on the composite and the star).
  - **Limits.** The site pass is split over dispatches of at most 2³⁰ taps (`DISPLAY_VIEW_QUALITY_SITE_TAPS_PER_DISPATCH`; a choice, untimed on hardware; results are the same for any split, tested). A kernel table above the device's storage binding limit is refused by name with `insufficient-limits`, never clipped. The GPU session now treats `insufficient-limits` thrown by a render like `unsupported-on-backend`: `"auto"` renders that part on the CPU, labelled, and `"require"` fails. The site pass uses no workgroup memory and binds at most seven storage buffers.
  - **New self-check case** `display-view:quality:checkerboard-13x9-defocus-blocked`: the engine's 6.3-site disc, the bilinear route, the blocked sum split over 18 dispatches. It runs with the two existing cases before a device's first display-view render.
  - **Measured agreement** (docs/WEBGPU.md, "Defocus on the GPU"): 30 fixtures on the float32 emulation and on SwiftShader. The 24-case grid covers focus 0.8× and 1.25×, f/2.8, f/8 and f/16, 6 µm and 1.4 µm, and Speed and Quality with diffraction. The other six are the composite under the label layer (Speed and Quality, and Quality at radius 94), a bar Siemens star, the disc at its 64-site cap, and a combined radius of 94. Every component is inside its bound, using at most 2.0 % of the expected-value bound and 2.8 % of the variance bound. ΔE2000 is at most 1.7 × 10⁻⁴ and |Δ|/σ at most 1.4 × 10⁻⁴. One sample of 17 712 differs, by one code. No pixel is flagged, and every case meets the #480 thresholds. **GPU timing: TBD-TIMING (#475)**; SwiftShader per-stage times are recorded, and they are not GPU performance.
  - Tests: `test/display-view-defocus-gpu.test.ts` (emulation: the 30 fixtures; the bound terms; the dispatch split; `supports()`; the route through `prepareDisplayView`; the `insufficient-limits` refusal and the session's fallback; the self-check; every assembled display-view shader declares no name twice), `test/webgpu/display-view.webgpu.ts` (SwiftShader: the 30 fixtures and the self-check), and the shared fixtures in `test/helpers/display-view-defocus-gpu-fixtures.ts`. Internal additions: `DisplayViewGpuSetup.defocusRadius`, the Quality result's `blockedSum`, `siteSumRelativeBound` and `latticePointsResolved`, and `stageMilliseconds.siteDispatches`.
- **Display view: the Quality kernels on the GPU (#479): diffraction kernels, the four-spot anti-aliasing filter, the AHD candidates and the low-signal correction, behind the same `prepareDisplayView(request, { gpu: "auto" | "require" })` renderer.** No result changes with the default `gpu: "off"`, no CPU reference byte changes (the display-view hash locks are unchanged), and none for requests the kernels do not cover (caller scene functions, coarser views, footprints above 6 real pixels per axis: the CPU reference, labelled `unsupported-on-backend`; `lens.defocus` out of focus is rendered since #492 step 2c, above). Every engine chart and declared `material-layers` scene is covered through #491's material modules, shared by both kernels; with diffraction the composite module is given the lattice groups and the reference's lattice formula as its query lattice. The renderer implements `dispose()`. `DisplayViewGpuRequest` gains `defocus` (#492: true when `lens.defocus` resolves a non-zero disc; the renderer gathers it since #492 step 2c, above). The GPU session now registers one renderer, `createDisplayViewGpuRenderer` (id `photivra-display-view`), that runs the Speed kernel for requests it covers and the Quality passes for the rest. `src/gpu/wgsl-display-view-quality-kernel.ts` has three passes per band of display rows: the lattice materials of every lattice group (offsets sharing a fractional shift, as the reference groups them) through the material module; the site moments, gathering each site's expected electrons over every tap of every group from a table of the reference's own combined kernels per material and channel (formed on the host in float64 from the expected signal's tables; 400 taps per site at f/8 on 6 µm), then the readout moments and the white clip; and the display pixels, running the reference's per-real-pixel loop with both AHD candidates' full channel covariance, development, the Stein covariance, the residual, AHD's choice variance and the correction's trilinear interpolation. **The correction states stay on the host (#447):** the kernel records every grid key it needs in a bitmap and flags pixels whose state is missing; the host computes those states with the reference's `computeDisplayViewCorrectionStates`, uploads the sorted table and dispatches the band's pixel pass again; the renderer keeps the states per correction key across renders. The host finishes every pixel with the reference's own float64 functions, so **the noise draw is bit-identical to the CPU's**, as in Speed. Flagged display pixels (an uncertain lattice material whose effect could exceed 2⁻¹⁰ of a site's electrons, a correction-threshold decision within its bound, a missing state, a non-finite value) are recomputed with the reference. New self-check case `display-view:quality:checkerboard-13x9-diffraction-ahd` (diffraction, four spots, AHD and the correction on a 38 × 26 frame).
  - **Bounds** (docs/WEBGPU.md, "Display view (Quality) on the GPU"): the AHD kernels and the colour matrix mix signs, so the kernels carry every quantity as a pair (value, absolute bound) with first-order rules from the WGSL accuracy table (E = 2⁻²³ per + − ×, division 2.5 ULP, sqrt 4.5 ULP, log2 2⁻²¹ or 3 ULP), the clipped-normal moments propagate their inputs with their exact first-order derivatives and add the Speed clip constants, and the host doubles the per-pixel bound and adds the output quantization term's dependence on the mean (dq/dm ≤ 6.04·Δ²/12). Reported as `relative-to-reference` with the relative term 2·2⁻⁴⁰ and the largest per-pixel absolute term.
  - **Measured agreement** (float32 emulation in every CI run, SwiftShader in `test:webgpu`; nine small fixtures with diffraction, the four-spot filter and AHD, 82 215 components each): every component inside its bound using at most 3.0 % of the expected-value bound and 9.2 % of the variance bound (the white-clip fixture; below 1 % elsewhere except Reinhard 1.7 % and AHD without diffraction 2.3 %); ΔE2000 at most 4.1 × 10⁻⁴, at most 7.6 × 10⁻⁴ noise standard deviations, σ within 3 × 10⁻³ relative at the white clip and 8 × 10⁻⁵ elsewhere; at most 7 samples of 82 215 differ, by one code; the corrected-pixel count equals the reference's. The whole 24 MP 6 µm frame at 2028 × 1352 (checkerboard, f/8; emulation, run once): ΔE2000 at most 3.3 × 10⁻⁴, 5.4 × 10⁻⁴ σ, 1 035 of 8.2 million samples differ by one code, 0.43 % / 0.53 % of the bounds used; CI runs a full-width 32-row band of it.
  - **AHD placement, measured; inconclusive without hardware** (docs/WEBGPU.md, the per-stage table): on SwiftShader, Google's Vulkan implementation on the CPU (not GPU performance), every pass is slower than one CPU thread running the reference in the same browser (the expected signal 1.0–1.1 s against 72–274 ms; the AHD pixel pass 2.0–2.4 s against 0.32–0.81 s per 27 405-pixel view), so only hardware can decide. The CPU numbers alone bound the alternative: the stage after the expected signal is 57–85 % of the warm CPU render (81 % on the app's Quality request), so putting only the expected signal on the GPU caps the speed-up at 1.2–1.75×. The AHD stage therefore runs on the GPU; the "signal on the GPU, AHD on the CPU" path is not built (it needs a bound for the reference loop's sensitivity to the GPU's electrons) and is the next step if the hardware run (#475) shows the pixel pass slower than the CPU. The correction states, the finishing and flagged pixels stay on the CPU. **Hardware timing: TBD-TIMING (#475).**
  - **Known Quality limits are inherited:** the GPU reproduces the estimate, including its excess colour noise at small footprints (#379) and the star-centre AHD colour miss (#457).
  - Tests: `test/display-view-quality-gpu.test.ts` (emulation: the nine fixtures, the states kept across renders, flagged pixels, banding and regions, the support check, the kernels' assembly, the combined renderer through `prepareDisplayView({ gpu: "auto" })`, the self-check), `test/display-view-quality-gpu-full.test.ts` (a full-width 32-row band of the 24 MP frame at 2028 × 1352; the whole view with `PHOTIVRA_DISPLAY_VIEW_WHOLE=1`), `test/webgpu/display-view.webgpu.ts` (SwiftShader: the nine fixtures with the CPU's per-stage times in the same realm, banding and regions, the self-check). Internal additions: `displayViewSignalTables` (the expected signal's read-only tables), the AHD grid constants and the Speed host's `queryGeometry`, `gaussLegendre16` and `axisRecord` are exported for the Quality host; `DisplayViewGpuMaterialModule` is unchanged.
- **The #480 preview acceptance harness runs the app's request on the GPU (`--gpu`; `npm run accept:gpu`, `npm run accept:gpu:hardware`).** Tooling only; no result changes. A GPU producer renders the request in headless Chromium (the WebGPU suite's driver, SwiftShader by default, the hardware adapter with `--hardware`) through `prepareDisplayView(request, { gpu: "require", gpuDevice })` on the built engine, records the execution label (backend, adapter, conformance, fallback reason) and fails the case loudly when the GPU did not render it. The GPU is judged against the CPU reference rendered in the same browser page (`--reference browser`, the default with `--gpu`), because Chromium's and Node's `Math.atan2` differ in the last bit (#471) and move lattice points on a Siemens star's sector boundaries; Node's reference is still rendered and its difference reported per case. The app's request is given in `material-layers` form (`--scene material-layers`, the default) with a generic engine-authored stand-in label layer (`standInLabelLayer`; not the app's artwork), and the cases now include the app's composite chart and the same request out of focus (`app-defocus`, reported as an expected fallback until #492 step 2c), next to the five fixtures, in Speed and Quality; `--region` compares one region of every view. Internal: `renderPreparedDisplayViewWithInternals` (`src/facade/simulate-display-view.ts`, not exported from the package) returns a prepared view's render with its expected values and noise σ; the suite's loopback server also serves `dist/` and `scripts/lib/`. Docs: `docs/DISPLAY_VIEW.md` and `docs/WEBGPU.md`, "Preview acceptance" (the owner's command for a Mac). Tests: `test/display-view-acceptance.test.mjs`, `test/display-view-prepared-internals.test.ts`.
- **Display view: `lens.defocus` out of focus is rendered (#492, step 2b, the CPU display view).** No result changes without the option or in focus: every display-view hash lock holds. The display view and the capture (step 2a) apply the same per-node kernels.
  - **How:** the disc enters the expected-signal tables as a site kernel (`buildExpectedSignalInput`'s new `defocus` input, the kernels built as the capture builds them), so every query stays on the site lattice and both estimators run unchanged on the blurred lattice signal. Without diffraction every spectral node takes the disc itself (the view then reads the scene with the lattice formula, as with diffraction); with `lens.diffraction` node λ takes its diffraction kernel convolved with the disc (`convolveSiteKernels`, radius R_diff + R_defocus), a further labelled approximation. The new `view.defocus` assumption records the kernel and its radii. Prepared views build the combined kernels and tables once per request; regions stay byte-identical to the whole view.
  - **Limits:** those of the capture: the disc's cap of 64 sites (a larger disc fails, naming the cap and the radius) and the diffraction cap of 32, so a combined radius of at most 96 sites. Lattice points beyond the 64 columns the lattice rings keep are looked up directly (same values, higher cost).
  - **GPU:** the Speed-only renderer refuses a view whose expected signal carries site kernels at render time (`gpu: "auto"` then renders on the CPU, labelled `unsupported-on-backend`); the engine's combined renderer gathers the kernels since step 2c (above).
